# Oddkin Implementation Plan

Oddkin is an original, modular frontend framework built on custom elements and browser APIs. It includes a reusable design system, an independent token pipeline, optional application services, optional developer tooling (analyzer, Workbench with Theme Studio, agent tooling), and a separate documentation application that consumes the published packages. Public names ([ADR 0037](./docs/architecture/decisions/0037-naming-contract.md)): packages `@oddkin/*`; custom elements `<odd-*>`; CSS custom properties (tokens and component variables) `--odd-*`; theme-scope attributes `data-odd-*`; JavaScript `Oddkin*` / `oddkin*` / `ODDKIN_*`; command-line tools `oddkin`, `oddkin-tokens`, `oddkin-analyze`, `oddkin-agent`; files `oddkin.*`; schemas under `https://oddkin.dev/`. Rule of thumb: HTML and CSS use `odd`, everything else uses `oddkin`.

The aim is to make correct, maintainable components easy to write and easy to document: clear ownership, predictable state, stable DOM, simple cancellation, accessible behavior, useful defaults, and documentation generated from the real contracts. Performance and usability improvements are **hypotheses to verify**, not claims established by this document.

**Developer assurance objective.** Oddkin helps teams author and customize components, understand why they behave and appear as they do, assess the consequences of changes, and verify that supported customizations preserve required behavior. Intended outcome: teams can customize shared Oddkin components, determine which products a change may affect, run the relevant verification, inspect supported explanations, and export reproducible failures through one coherent set of contracts and optional tools (§5h). These capabilities are optional development tooling; production runtime size, startup, dependencies, and boundaries are unchanged.

**Legend:** `[ ]` todo · `[x]` implemented **and** verified · `[~]` in progress · `[!]` blocked (add note). A milestone is complete only with its acceptance evidence. Once the workspace exists, every milestone leaves packages buildable and the current docs/Workbench slice runnable; before the docs slice exists, keep a working plain-HTML consumer.

## Priority plan (round 10, from 2026-10-28)

**Owner direction (2026-10-28).** Work in priority order. First come the foundations people need to install Oddkin and build with it, working together as one system. Then Workbench and the AI tooling must work end to end. Then the remaining foundations, and only then new capability, starting with the SVG icon packages. The milestones (M0–M12) and the numbered sections stay as the detailed backlog and the evidence record; this section sets the order and the owners. Take an item when its tier is reached, or earlier when an item in a higher tier depends on it. Tick an item here and in the milestone item it names.

**Lanes** (log, tools, and prompts in `C:\SLT_Backup_10-21-2026\oddkin-lanes`: `lanes-r10.md`, `prompts/round-10/`):

| Lane | Owns |
| --- | --- |
| Agent 1, runtime foundations and release | `@oddkin/core`, `state`, `app`, `tokens`, and `components` (every element; not `src/composition/`); the CSS recipes and compatibility data; size budgets; packed consumers; CI; releases. Later `@oddkin/icons` |
| Agent 2, tooling | `@oddkin/workbench` (Theme Studio and the panels other sites embed), `@oddkin/analyzer`, `@oddkin/ai`, the composition schema, the agent kits and `llms.txt`, and the AI examples |
| Agent 3, documentation | the docs site (`apps/docs`, its workflow and tests). Figma fidelity comes first, then content that is accurate and covers every package, including the package READMEs |

Agents 1 and 2 no longer work on the docs site. They answer Agent 3's questions and fix, in their own packages, what Agent 3 reports.

### P0: people can install Oddkin and build with it (now)

Exit criteria:
- In a clean directory, every adoption path below builds and runs from the published beta, and in CI from packed tarballs.
- Workbench documents a component library with live, editable previews.
- The AI elements stream from a real endpoint.
- CI runs the whole gate on every push.
- The owner has published 1.0.0-beta.1.

- [ ] **P0.1 Fresh-install matrix (Agent 1).**
  - Build scripted consumers in temporary directories, from the registry's beta and from packed tarballs:
    - plain HTML with an import map;
    - a Vite TypeScript app;
    - tokens as CSS only, and in a Sass build (the tokens package has no Sass output; Sass styles read the custom properties);
    - core only;
    - components only, with the autoloader and with explicit `define` imports;
    - a full app with the router, state, a form, and an AI element;
    - React, Vue, Svelte, and Angular apps;
    - `oddkin-tokens` building a theme config.
  - Each page must render, register its elements, submit its form, and log no console error, in three engines.
  - Fix every failure where it starts. Failures in another lane's package go to that lane with a reproduction.
  - Covers M12 "Independent consumers…", M11 "Every copied/generated example and exported theme verified in fresh directories…", and M1 "Node tooling's required compiler/build dependencies…".
  - *(2026-10-28, Agent 1: fresh Vite TypeScript (with Sass), React, Vue, and Svelte apps install the packed packages and their own build tools, type-check (TypeScript, TSX), build with Vite, and pass in three engines: they register the elements, report typed input, and submit the form with no console error (`tests/consumers/`, `e2e/fresh-consumers.spec.ts`, `scripts/packed-consumers.mjs`). The full app now also uses an AI element (`odd-suggestions` fills the form). `ODDKIN_CONSUMERS_FROM=<dist-tag>` installs from the registry instead (`.github/workflows/registry-smoke.yml`). Remaining: an Angular app (typings only so far) and the registry run's first result.)*
- [ ] **P0.2 Cross-package compatibility (Agent 1).**
  - Close §2.5's open items: two independently built consumers and duplicate core copies.
  - Make version ranges compatible across the packages, token outputs, metadata, configuration, and frames.
  - Make dependency declarations install a single core, and add duplicate-version diagnostics.
  - Covers M12 "Verify installed Node tool dependencies, optional framework declarations/adapters, duplicate-version diagnostics…".
  - *(2026-10-28, Agent 1: one copy per install. `@oddkin/core` is a peer dependency of components, state, app, and ai; `@oddkin/state` of app; `@oddkin/components` of ai; `@oddkin/tokens` stays a dependency (release-process.md §1, `tests/architecture/single-core.test.ts`). Development builds record each copy of core under `Symbol.for('oddkin.core.copies')`, and the second copy warns once (`[core.copies]`) with both versions; production builds carry none of it (core-contracts.md §5.1; `packages/core/test/copies.browser.test.ts`, `e2e/duplicate-core.spec.ts`). Remaining: released packages installed at two versions on one page, realms other than Workbench frames, and the share payload and frame protocol ranges with Agent 2.)*
- [ ] **P0.3 Workbench in working order (Agent 2).**
  - **The consumer path.** A library author installs the packed `@oddkin/workbench` and `@oddkin/analyzer` and writes `oddkin.config.ts`. `oddkin workbench dev` and `oddkin workbench build` then work against an Oddkin library and against a standard-CEM fixture library.
  - **Component pages.** Each renders the real component in its frame. Editing attributes, properties, slots, and CSS custom properties in the API table updates the preview in place. Presets, Reset, the events log, and Copy example work, and the catalog's live previews load.
  - **The owner's report (2026-10-28).** On the docs site's component pages, the real components and their examples do not reliably load or update when the table changes.
    - Reproduce it in Workbench and in the embedded panels, and fix it in the panels and the frame runtime.
    - Give Agent 3 a written embedding contract: the files to serve, the frame document, the policy, and the panel properties.
  - **Theme Studio's baseline flow** works: load the library's theme, edit it, preview light and dark, and export.
  - **Watch mode** survives renames and deletions.

- [ ] **P0.4 The AI tooling in working order (Agent 2).**
  - **Streaming from a real endpoint.** `@oddkin/ai`, installed from the packed beta in a fresh app, streams from a real endpoint through the AI SDK UI message stream adapter.

**Every tier.**
- Each file has one owner, the lane in the table above; post before changing another lane's file.
- Each slice keeps the whole system green: CI, the packed consumers, and (from P0.5) the golden path.
- Evidence goes in `docs/verification.md`. Nothing is ticked without evidence.

## 1. Research synthesis (summary)

Full table and sources: [docs/research/synthesis.md](./docs/research/synthesis.md). Reviewed: Lit, FAST, Web Awesome/Shoelace, Spectrum WC, Material Web, UI5 WC, Stencil/Ionic, React, Angular, Vue, Svelte, Solid.

1. **Ownership is the central abstraction.** One `Scope` owns listeners, timers, effects, observers, subscriptions, and child work. Connections, effect runs, and requests have different lifetimes.
2. **One reactive model** for local signals, public properties, store selectors, context, and resources.
3. **Targeted updates are ordinary.** Cached static templates, concrete binding parts, preserved node identity.
4. **The default authoring path is small** — base class, signal, template, registration. No provider, reducer, decorator transform, compiler, or theme generator required.
5. **A complete system through optional modules** that share one ownership protocol; components remain usable from any framework.
6. **Delivery and documentation are framework features** — exports, no-build examples, errors, metadata, generated docs, migrations, token tooling.
7. **Prove improvements** with reproducible evidence, including unfavorable results.

**Differentiation thesis:** a coherent combination of an understandable browser-based authoring core, explicit automatic cleanup, binding-level reactivity, first-class token tooling, documentation and Theme Studio generated from real contracts, and optional application services with compatible semantics. The ideas exist individually elsewhere; implementation and evidence must establish the value of the combination.

---

## 2. Product and package contracts

### 2.1 Packages and dependencies

| Package | Responsibility | Internal deps |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `@oddkin/core` | Reactive primitives, `Scope`, element lifecycle, templates, styles, layout recipes, collections, models, registration | none |
| `@oddkin/tokens` | Theme config, pure generation, CSS/SCSS/JSON/TS outputs, CLI | none |
| `@oddkin/state` | Stores, selectors, context, persistence, inspection | core |
| `@oddkin/app` | App lifetime, router, resources, optional Node scaffolding | core, state |
| `@oddkin/components` | Accessible, themed custom elements, Oddkin layout presets, component models, published CEM + enrichment | core, tokens |
| `@oddkin/ai` (optional) | AI interaction records, DOM-free controllers, stream adapters, `odd-*` conversation/assistance/agent elements, published CEM + enrichment + record schemas (§5j) | core, tokens (refs), components (`odd-button`) |
| `@oddkin/analyzer` (optional, dev-only, Node) | Static analysis → normalized metadata → CEM, `oddkin.metadata.json`, Workbench indexes, editor and agent outputs; comparison snapshots and change impact (planned, §5h) | tokens (Node/DOM-free), core (DOM-free model types); compiler/parser dependencies confined to Node tooling |
| `@oddkin/workbench` (optional tooling) | Reusable metadata-driven documentation shell (Foundations, Components with integrated interactive examples, Theme Studio, optional Products and modes), page registry, isolated previews, `defineExamples`, `oddkin` CLI; portable conformance suites, explanations, and reproductions (planned, §5h) | browser: core, tokens, components; state/app integrated only after M6–M7; Node: analyzer, tokens/node; DOM-free examples/config entries independently buildable |
| `@oddkin/agent` (optional, dev-only, Node) | Project context, generated agent kits (entry guide, references, JSON, optional Agent Skills / `AGENTS.md` / `llms.txt` packaging), deterministic operations, CLI, optional MCP adapter | analyzer (API), tokens |
| `apps/docs` (private) | Product docs, tutorials, migrations, architecture; embeds Workbench panels | core, state, app, tokens, components, workbench panels — public exports only |

**Forbidden:** core → tokens; tokens → any Oddkin package; components → state/app/analyzer/workbench/agent; ai → state/app/analyzer/workbench/agent or any framework; any browser package, ordinary component consumer, or the docs runtime → `@oddkin/analyzer` or `@oddkin/agent`; ordinary component consumers → `@oddkin/workbench`; reverse deps; cycles; cross-package source aliases. Core is unbranded and works without Oddkin tokens.

**Runtime dependencies:** none from third parties in shipped browser code (browser packages, the Workbench browser bundle, the deployed docs app, published static Workbench builds). Node-only tooling (`@oddkin/analyzer`, `@oddkin/agent`, Workbench CLI/dev server) may use third-party tools (TypeScript compiler, Vite, test runners, Sass for fixtures). These are development tools for Oddkin consumers; a dependency needed when running an installed CLI must be bundled, declared in that tooling package’s `dependencies`, or supplied through a documented peer contract. Do not place a required CLI dependency only in unpublished `devDependencies`. Research/integration fixtures may install competing frameworks outside shipped code.

### 2.2 Import surfaces

Entry points per [packages.md](./docs/architecture/packages.md). Rules:

- Subpaths are created when implemented; a list of empty exports is not a delivered feature.
- `core/reactive`, `core/scope`, `core/layout`, `core/collections`, `core/model`, `state/store`, token browser entries (`theme`, `generate`, `refs`, `values`, `conditions`), and `workbench/examples`, `workbench/config`, `workbench/pages`, `workbench/content`, and the planned `workbench/conformance` are DOM-free. DOM-free is **not** an SSR renderer. The planned `core/inspect` entry resolves only under the `development` export condition and never appears in production graphs.
- Only explicit Node entries import `node:*`. Importing a class never defines an element; only `define` entries have registration side effects.
- Browser exports work from native ESM/import maps and packed tarballs without workspace aliases or an Oddkin build plugin.
- `package.json` of element packages declares `"customElements"`.

### 2.3 Developer questions the docs must answer

What does Oddkin provide? · Which packages do I need? · How do I install and use them? · How do I create a theme or component? · How do I lay out a component or page? · How do I build an application? · How do I document my own component library? · How do I adopt Oddkin incrementally? · How do I get my IDE agent to use Oddkin correctly? · How do I generate accurate agent support for my own library?

---

### 2.4 Capability ladder and supported integrations

Keep five independent entry paths explicit: tokens/CSS only; consume components in any app; author unbranded custom elements with core; opt into Oddkin state/application services; document a library with Workbench. Each path gets a minimal install, a plain-JavaScript example, a TypeScript example where relevant, and a packed-consumer verification.

- [x] Keep recipes, collections, model validators/schema emitters, persistence, inspection, and tooling out of the core counter and component-consumer graphs unless imported. Package ownership does not imply that every feature belongs in the root barrel. *(The `@oddkin/core` root re-exports only reactive, scope, template, styles, element, and diagnostics; layout, collections, model, helpers, controllers, and i18n are separate entries, and persistence lives in `@oddkin/state`. `node scripts/check-size.mjs` (`npm run size`) enforces this on the production graphs: no graph loads schema emitters, recipe inspection, the analyzer, Workbench, or TypeScript; the counter and typical component load no layout, collections, model, helpers, i18n, or controllers; the keyed list only its collection; the registered side navigation no layout runtime, collections, or model; and none of the core graphs loads state, app, or tokens. A used `collections` import added to the counter fixture fails it. ADR 0031.)*
- [x] Define a small interoperable readable/subscription contract with deterministic teardown. Document how external stores can connect without introducing a second Oddkin graph or requiring Oddkin stores. *(`ExternalSource<T>` (`getSnapshot`, `subscribe`) in `@oddkin/core/reactive`, the shape React's `useSyncExternalStore` takes; `@oddkin/ai` now uses the same type. `fromExternal(source)` reads a store as a `Readable`, subscribed only while observed and unsubscribed when the last observer leaves; `toExternal(readable)` goes the other way. [core-contracts.md](./docs/architecture/core-contracts.md) §5. `packages/core/test/external.test.ts` (7 tests): unobserved reads without a subscription, one shared subscription, unsubscribe on the last observer and on scope disposal, equality, batching, and no change missed at subscription. `tests/interop/external-store.browser.test.ts` passes in three engines: an Oddkin element renders a store and unsubscribes on disconnect, and React reads a signal through `useSyncExternalStore`. Imported, the helpers cost 168 B gzip; other graphs are unchanged. 2 of 2 mutants killed.)*
- [x] Generate opt-in event, tag-name, JSX, and editor declarations from the same contracts where each target supports them. Keep framework-specific declarations/adapters separate; native DOM compatibility does not automatically provide typed JSX or framework form integration. *(2026-10-19. Derived from the element classes, not copied from metadata text. **Events:** `ElementEvents<typeof Self>` maps `static events` (keyed by event name) to `CustomEvent<Detail>` or the native event type; each of the 23 elements with events declares it as a type-only `_events`, and `OddkinElement`'s `addEventListener`/`removeEventListener` read it, so every `@oddkin/ai` listener is typed with no global entries. `analyzer.event.key` reports a key that differs from its event name once `_events` is declared; `tests/types/typed-listeners.test.ts` requires the declaration. **Tag names:** every `define/*` entry references its element's declaration, so a registering import types `HTMLElementTagNameMap`. **Frameworks:** types-only entries `@oddkin/components/frameworks/react` (React 19 `JSX.IntrinsicElements`), `/vue` (`GlobalComponents`, Vue's documented pattern for custom elements: `@odd-action` as `onOddAction`, `v-model` as `modelValue`), and `/svelte` (`SvelteHTMLElements`, `on<event>` handler attributes) type every loaded `odd-*` tag from `ElementProperties` (settable properties, function-valued props included) and the element's event map (`ElementEventMap`). Checked by `npm run typecheck`: `tests/frameworks/react` (tsc), `tests/frameworks/vue` (vue-tsc, `strictTemplates`), and `tests/frameworks/svelte` (svelte-check: a clean file plus exactly the expected errors in `Mistakes.svelte`, because svelte-check ignores `@ts-expect-error` in markup); vue-tsc and svelte-check run from `tooling/framework-types` with TypeScript 5.9, because TypeScript 7 has no JavaScript API (ADR 0018). `verify-packs` type-checks all three from the installed tarballs. **Editor data:** the third-party VS Code custom-data generator already covers it from the CEM (`verify-packs`).)*
- [x] Cover rich object properties, event casing/detail, initial property detection, pre-definition values, remounts, and framework forms in pinned integration fixtures. Start with [React custom elements](https://react.dev/reference/react-dom/components#custom-html-elements), [Angular CUSTOM_ELEMENTS_SCHEMA](https://angular.dev/api/core/CUSTOM_ELEMENTS_SCHEMA), [Angular ControlValueAccessor](https://angular.dev/api/forms/ControlValueAccessor), [Vue](https://vuejs.org/guide/extras/web-components), and [Svelte](https://svelte.dev/docs/svelte/custom-elements). *(React 19, Vue 3.5, Angular 22.2, and Svelte 5.57 in `packages/components/test/frameworks*.browser.test.ts`, three engines: object properties, event names and details, remounts, and form bindings. Defined after the framework renders (`frameworks-late.browser.test.ts`): Angular `[items]`, Vue `.items`, and Svelte object values set the property, which survives the upgrade; React 19 and Vue without `.prop` write `items="[object Object],…"`, now reported in development as `element.attribute.propertyOnly`, and recover on the framework's next render. Initial property detection and pre-upgrade values: the contract harness for all 28 elements.)*

### 2.5 Compatibility and version coexistence

- [~] Publish a feature-by-feature browser/AT baseline, including fallbacks or explicit unsupported behavior for custom states, form association, adopted stylesheets, move-preserving DOM APIs, scoped registries, and query syntax. A standards draft is not evidence of shipped cross-browser support. *(Browser part done: [features.json](./docs/compatibility/features.json) and the generated [matrix](./docs/compatibility/matrix.md) record 69 CSS, DOM, and HTML features with floor support per subfeature, gates, baselines, floor exceptions, and evidence, checked by `npm run lint:css`; floor probe in Chrome 125 and Firefox 126 (§5i). Remaining: assistive-technology pairs (M8) and Safari 17.4 runtime evidence.)*
- [x] Registration is idempotent only for the same compatible definition. Never silently accept a different constructor/version already registered under the same name. Diagnose conflicts, document one-global-tag-set limits, and use isolated frames for incompatible Workbench versions. Custom prefixes are not a promise that an arbitrary constructor can be registered twice or its internal dependencies automatically renamed. *(2026-10-21: `define()` is a no-op for the same definition and otherwise throws `registry.definitionConflict`, `registry.constructorReused`, or `registry.collision` (`packages/core/test/definition.browser.test.ts`, `element.browser.test.ts`); a second copy of a component package gets `registry.collision` and the first registration stays (`e2e/duplicate-core.spec.ts`, three engines); the one-global-tag-set limit and why renaming tags does not help are in [core-contracts.md §5.1](./docs/architecture/core-contracts.md); Workbench frames reject incompatible registrations with `frame.registration.incompatible` (`packages/workbench/test/frame.browser.test.ts`).)*
- [~] Test two independently built consumers and compatible duplicate core installations; define which public protocols cross package copies/realms and which require matching versions. Avoid undocumented global singletons and `instanceof` assumptions at interoperability boundaries. *(2026-10-21: two esbuild bundles on one page, each with its own `@oddkin/core` and button module (`examples/duplicate-core`, `e2e/duplicate-core.spec.ts`, three engines). The contract is [core-contracts.md §5.1](./docs/architecture/core-contracts.md): DOM protocols and the external-store contract cross copies; readables do not, and development builds report `reactive.readable.foreign` where a template or helper binds one (`packages/core/test/foreign-readables.browser.test.ts`); errors cross by `code`, not `instanceof`. Remaining: released packages installed twice at different versions, and realms other than Workbench frames.)*
- [~] Define compatible version ranges and migrations across packages, token outputs, CEM/enrichment, config, examples, share payloads, and frame protocols. Use released artifacts for compatibility checks, not only workspace sources. *(2026-10-27, proposed in [release-process.md](./docs/release-process.md) §1: any `1.x` runtime packages work together; schemas carry their own versions and the previous one is read for one major; copies of core at any versions follow core-contracts.md §5.1. Remaining: share payloads and the frame protocol (unfrozen Workbench contracts), and checks against released artifacts once there is a release.)*

Research: [custom-element registration](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/custom-elements.html#dom-customelementregistry-define), [Node package exports](https://nodejs.org/api/packages.html), and the platform references in §12.3.
