## 3. Authoring model (summary)

Detailed contracts: [core-contracts.md](./docs/architecture/core-contracts.md) §1–§6.

- **Counter** needs `OddkinElement`, `signal`, `html`, `define`. `${this.count.value}` inside `render()` tracks a render-level dependency (render re-runs, deduplicated); `${this.count}` binds the part directly without re-running `render()`. Both use the same engine.
- **Properties** via `OddkinElement.with({ name: prop.string({...}) })` — typed, decorator-free, normal DOM accessors, metadata from the same definition (statically analyzable).
- **Lifecycle:** constructor (local initialization only; no external work) → `connected(scope)` → `mounted(scope)` → `updated(changes)` → `disconnected()`; plus `adopted`, `moved`, `updateComplete` with precise boundaries. Page suspension and back/forward cache restoration are not disconnects; application-level policy is an explicit `@oddkin/app` service (core-contracts.md §9.1, ADR 0035).
- **Scope:** `signal`, `listen`, `timeout`, `interval`, `add`, `child`, `effect`, `use`, `dispose`. No mirrored manual cleanup in normal code.
- **Reactivity:** synchronous `signal`/`computed`/`batch`; glitch-free; `Object.is` default; microtask-deduplicated commits; derivation → DOM → post-commit effects.
- **Interpretable APIs:** explicit definitions and dependencies, consistent terminology, strongly typed inputs and outputs in ordinary TS/JS/HTML/CSS, clear `Scope` ownership, one recommended approach per common task (alternatives with concrete reasons), and precise errors for ambiguous usage ([core-contracts.md](./docs/architecture/core-contracts.md) §1.2). Agent interpretability is a review criterion for every public API.
- **Templates:** text, attribute, `?bool`, `.prop`, `@event`, readables, nested templates; `when`, keyed `repeat`, `match`. No `eval` or implicit untrusted string-to-HTML conversion. Static tagged-template markup is parsed through an explicit CSP/Trusted Types-compatible path; dynamic strings become text by default. Strict CSP and Trusted Types tested.

### 3.1 Authoring semantics that must be explicit

- [x] Implement and document the binding rule (settled):
  - `render()` executes synchronously within a reactive tracking context. Readable `.value` accesses during that execution — including synchronous helper calls — register render-level dependencies. Changes schedule a deduplicated render.
  - Readables passed directly into template bindings (`${this.count}`) subscribe at the binding level and update without rerunning the enclosing `render()`.
  - Primitive values are snapshots. Reading `.value` outside a tracked context establishes no subscription. Deferred event handlers and asynchronous callbacks do not inherit the render's tracking context.
  - Internal reads used to initialize direct bindings must not register render-level dependencies. When a dependency participates in both paths, scheduling coalesces the work and avoids duplicate DOM writes.
  - Public properties declared with `prop.*` are readables for this purpose: reading them in `render()` tracks them; tests cover both paths.

  *(2026-10-27, reconciled with M3: [core-contracts.md](./docs/architecture/core-contracts.md) §1 "Binding rule"; `e2e/binding-rule.spec.ts` (plain JS and TS: direct, render-tracked, dual path coalesced, handler and async snapshots, public property, attribute) and `element.browser.test.ts` "binding rule in components" ([verification.md](./docs/verification.md) "M3 binding-rule walkthrough"); the remaining cases (reads in synchronous helpers tracked, a callback scheduled from `render()` untracked, a `prop.*` read in `render()` re-renders while one read through a bound `computed` does not) in `authoring-semantics.browser.test.ts`, three engines ([verification.md](./docs/verification.md) "§3.1–§3.2 reconciliation").)*
- [x] Publish a binding-value matrix for empty/null/undefined/unset, text, attributes, properties, booleans, classes/styles, listeners, nodes, and SVG. Specify removal versus empty-string behavior and whether functions are callbacks, explicit derivations, or unsupported values; never silently guess. *(2026-10-27, ADR 0046 D5: [core-contracts.md](./docs/architecture/core-contracts.md) §6.1; functions, symbols, and objects without their own string form are reported (`template.value.function`, `.symbol`, `.object`) and write nothing in child and attribute positions, a function in `?name` is reported, `.prop` assigns anything; `binding-values.browser.test.ts` in three engines, 12 of 12 probes killed ([verification.md](./docs/verification.md) "D5 binding-value matrix").)*
- [x] Specify when templates are created, mounted, updated, disconnected, resumed, and disposed; define node ownership and what happens when the same DOM node/template instance is supplied to multiple places. Document slot projection separately from generated child ownership. *(2026-10-27: [core-contracts.md](./docs/architecture/core-contracts.md) §6.1 "Node ownership" and "Template lifecycle", §4 lifetimes; `binding-values.browser.test.ts` "node ownership" (D5), `authoring-semantics.browser.test.ts` "template lifecycle" (instance reuse per call site, disposal on replacement, pause and resume with the same nodes, slotted children left to the page), and M3's `template.browser.test.ts` and `element.browser.test.ts` lifecycle tests.)*
- [x] Publish ordering examples for property writes, attributes, `batch`, `computed`, DOM commits, effects, `updated`, and `updateComplete`. Define nested updates, errors, detached writes, and whether readiness includes child elements. `updateComplete` must not imply that fonts, layout, network resources, or paint have completed. *(2026-10-27: [core-contracts.md](./docs/architecture/core-contracts.md) §6.1 "Ordering"; `ordering.browser.test.ts` (first connection; writes, attributes, `batch`, and computed values before and after the flush; a write in `updated()`; detached writes and reconnection; a render error; children not awaited), three engines.)*
- [x] Keep constructors free of network requests, global listeners, and connection-owned subscriptions. Allow legal local initialization and stable public property accessors so browser upgrades and framework property detection work. *(2026-10-27: [core-contracts.md](./docs/architecture/core-contracts.md) §3 "Constructors"; `authoring-semantics.browser.test.ts` "constructors" (no attributes, children, shadow root, or owned resources after construction; prototype accessors set before connection), M3's pre-upgrade replay, and the framework fixtures in `tests/frameworks`.)*
- [x] Make `Scope` examples cover external event targets, observers, timers, fetch signals, user-supplied signals, already-aborted signals, disposal during callbacks, and reconnect. A disposed connection gets a fresh scope; do not reuse an aborted signal. Distinguish cleanup, stopping observation, and cancellation of work that actually supports cancellation. *(2026-10-27: [core-contracts.md](./docs/architecture/core-contracts.md) §4 "Examples", each run in `authoring-semantics.browser.test.ts` "Scope examples" (three engines; WebKit 27.2 rejects an aborted `fetch` with its own `AbortError`, not the reason); reconnection in `failure-paths.browser.test.ts`; the API in `scope.test.ts`.)*
- [x] If custom template helpers/directives or controllers are public, give them a small typed extension contract for binding identity, updates, ownership, and teardown. Add an extension point only after two real use cases; ordinary functions and native CSS remain sufficient for simple composition. *(2026-10-27: [core-contracts.md](./docs/architecture/core-contracts.md) §6.1 "Attribute directives (the extension contract)" (four uses: `classMap`, `styleMap`, `ref`, `live`) and §4.1 controllers (eight); a third-party directive through the exported types in `authoring-semantics.browser.test.ts`. No public child-position extension: one use.)*
- [x] Specify event target/currentTarget, retargeting, propagation flags, cancellation, and listener options. Public state notifications are distinguishable from user intent; programmatic property assignments must not accidentally create framework feedback loops. *(2026-10-27: [core-contracts.md](./docs/architecture/core-contracts.md) §2 "Targets, propagation, and cancellation" and "No feedback loops", with `intent` (`user` / `notification`) and the contract suite's `not-programmatic` check; `authoring-semantics.browser.test.ts` "event semantics" and M3's listener-option tests in `helpers.browser.test.ts`.)*

Research: [Lit expressions](https://lit.dev/docs/templates/expressions/), [FASTElement](https://fast.design/docs/3.x/getting-started/fast-element/), [Solid fine-grained reactivity](https://docs.solidjs.com/advanced-concepts/fine-grained-reactivity), and [AbortController](https://dom.spec.whatwg.org/#interface-abortcontroller).

### 3.2 Failure handling and production diagnostics

- [x] Define error ownership for property conversion, derivations, effects, render/commit, cleanup, example setup, lazy imports, and asynchronous commands. Decide propagation versus an explicit owner error hook/fallback. Preserve unrelated scopes and make retry/reset behavior testable; do not imply that a Scope can intercept every detached promise rejection or native callback error. *(2026-10-27: [core-contracts.md](./docs/architecture/core-contracts.md) §6.2 (the table, verified by M3's `failure-paths.browser.test.ts`; "Owners outside core": resources, commands, lazy routes, the autoloader, Workbench's example setup; "What a `Scope` cannot catch"). The autoloader's failed import was an unhandled rejection cached for good; it is now `autoload.import.failed` and `load()` tries again (`e2e/autoload.spec.ts`).)*
- [x] Use stable error codes and source/binding/phase context. Development diagnostics should explain a fix; production preserves essential failures without shipping full inspectors, source content, debug histories, or expensive validation by default. Test development and production builds. *(M3, ADR 0031: `development` → `dist/`, default → `dist/prod/`; production keeps `[code]`, behaviors and security checks; phase and owner are in the code (`element.<hook>.error`, `reactive.effect.cleanupError`) and `origin`; `packages/core/test/production.test.ts`.)*
- [~] Provide opt-in bounded graph/lifetime inspection using existing ownership records rather than a second reactive engine. Keep logs, histories, caches, and observers bounded and removable. *(2026-10-27, [core-contracts.md](./docs/architecture/core-contracts.md) §6.2 "Inspection": lifetime inspection is `scopeStats()` (both builds; the lifetime benchmark), store inspection a bounded log (`@oddkin/state/inspect`), and the caches are bounded. Remaining: per-readable graph inspection and commit causes, `@oddkin/core/inspect`, owned by M11's runtime explanations (after v1 in the §11 proposal).)*
- [x] Batch writes and avoid hidden layout reads during binding commits. Any measurement helper must state its scheduling and cleanup; do not introduce preemptive scheduling or a compiler through this requirement. *(M3: `failure-paths.browser.test.ts` spies layout getters, `getBoundingClientRect`, and `getComputedStyle` during direct updates (none read) and `element.browser.test.ts` coalesces a dual-path value into one write; the measuring controllers state their timing (`ResizeController` `immediate`/`frame`, `PositionController`'s placement frame; core-contracts.md §4.1).)*

---

### 3.3 Component composition and repository organization (planned — not yet implemented)

Oddkin's own component implementations use **one folder per component**, assembled through the **same public composition API** that consumers use. Consumers may keep colocated authoring (class, template, and styles in one module, as in `examples/core-counter` today); both paths are supported and stay exercised. No source files have been reorganized yet; the API and migration are M4 work (see M4 "Composition API and component folders").

```text
card/
  card.ts             element class: properties, state, methods, lifecycle
  card.template.ts    external template (type-only import of the host class)
  card.styles.ts      component styles
  card.definition.ts  compose(): class + template + styles + optional dependencies
  card.define.ts      explicit browser registration — the only file with side effects
  index.ts            side-effect-free public exports
```

```ts
// card/index.ts — never registers the component or its dependencies
export { Card } from './card.js';
export { cardDefinition } from './card.definition.js';

// card/card.define.ts — registration stays explicit
import { define } from '@oddkin/core/element';
import { cardDefinition } from './card.definition.js';

define(cardDefinition);
```

- Component-specific tests, examples (`card.examples.ts`), fixtures, and authored documentation live in or beside the folder where the repository's tooling supports it. Extra files are added only for a concrete purpose.
- `index.ts` exposes the supported public API and public types. Templates and styles are exported from it only when they are intentionally supported customization APIs.
- Files inside a component import their siblings directly, never through their own `index.ts`, which can create cycles. External templates use `import type` for their host class where appropriate.
- Package entries map onto the folders: `@oddkin/components/card` → `card/index.ts`; `@oddkin/components/define/card` → `card/card.define.ts`. Ordinary barrels never import a `*.define.ts` file.
- Recomposable components (§5g) add `card.structural.styles.ts` and a skin-free `foundation.ts` barrel reached through `@oddkin/components/card/foundation`; exporting a template or styles is a deliberate compatibility commitment.

## 4. State, resources, applications (summary)

Detailed contracts: [core-contracts.md](./docs/architecture/core-contracts.md) §7–§9.

- **Stores:** `createStore(initial)`, `update`, memoized `select`, read-only snapshots; optional action instrumentation; context, persistence, inspection as separate imports.
- **Resources:** inert construction; `scope.use(resource)`; source-driven generations with abort **and** generation guards; discriminated `idle | pending | success | error`; opt-in retry/cache/dedupe; reads only — mutations are explicit commands.
- **App & router:** `createApp({ root, setup })` with idempotent `start`/`stop`; URL/History/anchors/`import()`; typed params; focus/title/scroll/announcements; base path; hash mode for static hosts. SSR, hydration, compiler, preemptive scheduling are deferred.

### 4.1 State and application adoption contracts

- [x] Document immutable snapshot semantics for nested objects, arrays, maps/sets, equality, and unsupported values. Provide one clear recommended update pattern; freeze/validation policy must not silently change behavior between development and production. *([core-contracts.md](./docs/architecture/core-contracts.md) §7.1 "Snapshot semantics": one recommended update (a new snapshot that keeps unchanged parts by reference); plain objects and arrays frozen at any depth in both builds; maps, sets, dates, and class instances held by reference and replaced to change; identity equality, no proxies. The freeze policy is the same in development and production (ADR 0031), with a per-store opt-out. Tests in `packages/state/test/store.test.ts`.)*
- [x] External subscriptions have stable snapshots, selected-value equality, reentrancy rules, and explicit teardown. Context keys are typed, provider precedence is deterministic, and missing-provider behavior is actionable. *(Store subscriptions: stable snapshots, selected-value equality, reentrancy rules, explicit teardown (core-contracts.md §7.1, `store.test.ts`). Context (§7.2, `context.browser.test.ts`): typed keys matched by identity, the nearest provider in the composed tree with nested overrides and a host's own requests skipped, and missing providers as a `fallback` with `provided` false plus the `state.context.missing` report for `required` consumers, which then resolve when a provider appears.)*
- [x] Persistence is an optional versioned adapter with explicit selection, migration/reset, and storage-failure handling. It does not automatically persist every public component property. *(`@oddkin/state/persist`, a separate import ([core-contracts.md](./docs/architecture/core-contracts.md) §7.3): nothing is stored unless the whole state is opted in or a `select`ion is given with a `restore`; `{ version, data }` envelopes with step-by-step `migrate`, `validate` for untrusted stored data, `blocked` newer data, and `clear()`; unreachable storage, read, parse, migration, validation, serialization, and quota failures are reported once per failing streak and never reach the store, verified with real Web Storage in three engines.)*
- [x] Distinguish aborting a read from undoing a server mutation. Commands define pending/error/result ownership; automatic retries of mutations require an explicit policy. Avoid adding a mandatory query cache, middleware stack, or action ceremony to the basic state path. *(`@oddkin/app/command` ([core-contracts.md](./docs/architecture/core-contracts.md) §8.1): a command's runs belong to the scope it is connected to, its `idle`/`pending`/`success`/`error`/`cancelled` state to the command, and each `run()` resolves its own outcome without rejecting. Cancelling, or the owner ending, aborts the attempt and stops waiting; the state says `cancelled`, never `idle`, because the server may have applied it. Nothing is retried without `retry: { retries, when, delay? }`, whose `when` names the failures that are safe to repeat; `exclusive` concurrency by default drops double submissions, and no policy aborts a running mutation to start another. Independent of stores: no cache, middleware, or action ceremony. Tests in `packages/app/test/command.test.ts` and `command.browser.test.ts`.)*
- [x] Route fixtures cover native anchors, modifiers/download/external links, back/forward, query/hash changes, nested route errors, lazy-load failures, rapid navigation, and stop/restart. Locale and deployment base path are explicit inputs rather than global assumptions. *(`packages/app/test/router.browser.test.ts` (9 tests × 3 engines, the real History API): native anchors in light and open shadow DOM; Ctrl, Meta, Shift, Alt, middle-button, `target`, `download`, `rel="external"`, other-origin, outside-base, fragment, and already-handled clicks left alone; back and forward; query changes reloading only levels that declare `search`; fragment-only changes reloading nothing; a failing nested level under a ready parent; a failed `import()`; a superseded navigation aborted with one history entry left; stop and restart with the application. `e2e/router.spec.ts` on `examples/router` (Chromium, Firefox, WebKit) lets the browsers act: a real `target="_blank"` tab, a real download, a `rel="external"` document inside the base path, browser back and forward, and reloads in history and hash mode. A real Ctrl+click opens another page in Chromium and WebKit; it is skipped in Firefox, where Playwright did not reliably report the opened page (the router's decision is covered there by the fixtures). `base` is an explicit option (default `/`), and the router reads no locale: titles and announcements are the application's words.)*
- [x] Page suspension and restoration (core-contracts.md §9.1, ADR 0035): distinguish element disconnect/reconnect, application stop/restart, page suspension, back/forward cache restoration, and permanent disposal; never dispose on `pagehide`; lifecycle listeners only through an explicit owned app service; declared service suspend/resume; resource `revalidateOnRestore` policy with stale-result guards; no automatic mutation retries; no `unload`; `freeze`/`resume` only as verified enhancements. Owned by M7. *(`pageLifecycle(app)` in `@oddkin/app/lifecycle`, used explicitly in `setup`, registers `pagehide`/`pageshow`/`visibilitychange` once on the run's scope, plus `freeze`/`resume` where the browser has them; a persisted `pagehide` or a `freeze` calls `app.suspend()`, which calls the services' `suspend()` in reverse order, and a persisted `pageshow` resumes them in order; nothing is disposed, a non-persisted `pagehide` does nothing, and no `unload`/`beforeunload` listener exists. Resources with `revalidateOnRestore` load again after a restoration (unless fresh by `freshFor`), superseding a load pending from before the suspension; commands are never retried. Element disconnects, stop/restart, reloads, and in-page history stay distinct (§9.1 table). Tests: `lifecycle.test.ts` (16 Node tests), `lifecycle.browser.test.ts` (3 × 3 engines with real `PageTransitionEvent`s, including that importing every core, components, state, and app entry registers no lifecycle listener), `e2e/lifecycle.spec.ts`; 38 of 38 mutants killed (34 against the Node and browser tests, 4 against the e2e after rebuilding), three of them only after their first survival strengthened the tests; ADR 0035 accepted.)*

Research: [History API](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/nav-history-apis.html#the-history-interface), [Fetch and abort](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/), and [DOM AbortController](https://dom.spec.whatwg.org/#interface-abortcontroller).

---

## 5. Tokens, theming, styling (summary)

Detailed design: [theming-and-styling.md](./docs/architecture/theming-and-styling.md).

- `defineTheme()` (TS) and JSON + schema → one resolved model shared by files, CLI, and Theme Studio.
- Precedence: Oddkin defaults → preset → user config → explicit named overrides. Provenance recorded per token.
- Pipeline: load/validate → defaults/preset → scales/palettes → aliases → theme/product/density composition → context validation → emit. Pure, deterministic, browser-compatible; Node I/O separate. **The only token engine** — equivalent resolved inputs and emitter versions produce byte-identical canonical token artifacts in CLI and browser execution. Environment-specific paths, timing, and diagnostic reports are separate from canonical artifacts.
- Spacing: `multiples` | `ratio` | `explicit`, mutually exclusive; 4-decimal rounding in source unit; stable names; semantic roles alias scale steps explicitly.
- Colour: OKLCH ramps + documented sRGB gamut mapping; WCAG checks on actual role pairs; APCA/CVD labelled supplementary.
- Outputs: CSS (no JS needed), typed `refs`, separate literal `values`, `inspect` with explicit element context, Sass refs + literals, DTCG 2025.10 JSON, provenance metadata, validation reports.
- Scopes via `data-odd-theme|mode|product|density|profile`; each scope re-declares the affected alias closure. Research: [CSS custom properties](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-variables-1/) and [Shadow DOM styling/inheritance](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-shadow-1/).
- Styling: CSS custom properties are the contract; plain `.css`, CSS-in-TS (`css` + `token()`), and Sass all supported; one constructed sheet per style definition per document; `<style>` fallback with CSP nonce; bounded `::part` / component-variable surface.
- Token categories: foundation → semantic layout roles → component roles, plus **named conditions** (literal thresholds) and **profiles** (explicit role overrides). Responsive layout, density, typography preference, and target size stay distinct.

### 5.1 Theme portability and scope correctness

- [x] Keep spacing strategy semantics discoverable: a base plus explicit factors, or a base/ratio with counts before and after it, or an explicit scale. Define counts, rounding, zero/negative values, bounds, and extension behavior. Avoid ambiguous `default`/`multiplier`/`range` fields; semantic/t-shirt names are explicit aliases rather than automatically renumbered identities. *(2026-10-21, [theming-and-styling.md](./docs/architecture/theming-and-styling.md) §4: fields and rules per strategy (factors ≥ 0, ratio > 0 and ≠ 1, 0–12 steps per side, explicit values as written including 0 and negative), 4-decimal rounding in the source unit, offset-based names that never renumber when steps are added; the steps preset roles alias are a stated requirement, reported once as `spacing.missingStep` at `/spacing`. `spacing-units.test.ts`; 5 of 5 mutants killed.)*
- [x] `rem` outputs respect the consumer's root size. Never force a global root font size or silently assume a pixel conversion. Distinguish CSS references from literals, preserved units from observed pixels, and context-dependent values from pure generated results. *(2026-10-21, theming-and-styling.md §3: generated CSS declares only `--odd-*` properties and `color-scheme`; `rem` stays `rem` in CSS, `values`, inspection, and DTCG; px-vs-rem target checks report `target.unitMismatch` instead of converting; references, generated literals (per mode and per `resolveToken` context), and observed pixels (`getComputedStyle` only) are documented separately. `spacing-units.test.ts`; `e2e/tokens-css-only.spec.ts` scales every `rem` token with a 20 px root in three engines.)*
- [x] Test CSS-only system-mode selection and first paint, explicit mode overrides, nested mode/product/density/profile combinations, inherited aliases, consumer overrides, forced colors, and user font-size preferences. Any reset, font loading, or page defaults are explicit opt-in assets. *(System mode, explicit overrides, nested scopes, aliases, and root font size: `e2e/tokens-css-only.spec.ts` (M2). Consumer overrides across layers and scopes: `cascade.browser.test.ts`; forced colours and 200% text with components: `e2e/css-compat.spec.ts`, `styling.browser.test.ts` (§5i). 2026-10-21: first paint: an init script records every animation frame from document start; in Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit the first frame already has the emulated light or dark scheme and body background (first-contentful-paint reported per engine, indicative only). Forced colours on the CSS-only page in Chromium and Firefox: system colours replace token colours, token sizes still apply, the action keeps a visible boundary (fixed: it had `border: 0`) and its focus outline; WebKit emulates only the media query and is skipped with that reason. See [verification.md](./docs/verification.md).)*
- [ ] Adopted stylesheet sharing follows document ownership; externally supplied mutable sheets have explicit ownership rules. A preview-owned mutable theme sheet must not become a shared component static sheet.
- [x] Define legal composition/precedence and generated CSS size for multiple scope axes. Measure selector/rule growth before generating every possible cross-product. Report conflicting or redundant overrides with provenance. *(2026-10-21, [theming-and-styling.md](./docs/architecture/theming-and-styling.md) §5.4: precedence table for nested scopes, several axes on one element (emission order products → densities → profiles; profiles win overlaps), profile lists, nested themes, and overrides. No cross-product rules are generated: with 1/2/3/10 of every axis the CSS grows by exactly the sum of each axis alone (default theme 26,326 → 68,771 B raw, 2,720 → 4,924 B gzip at 10 of each). Conflicts: `profile.overlap`; redundant settings: `override.redundant`, `profile.redundant`, `product.redundant`, `density.redundant`, each at its configuration path, and the output equals the configuration without them. Tests: `composition.test.ts`, `scopes-shadow.browser.test.ts` (all axes on one element, three engines); 5 of 5 mutants killed. Not detected: duplicate keys inside one JSON object.)*
- [x] DTCG output uses the pinned format's actual typed representation and documents Oddkin extensions, supported token types, aliases, and lossless versus lossy mappings. An Oddkin resolved-theme JSON file is not automatically a valid DTCG document; verify with an independent reader. DTCG import/round-trip is a separate, explicit scope decision. *(2026-10-21, [theming-and-styling.md](./docs/architecture/theming-and-styling.md) §6.1: `emitDtcg(theme, context)` in `@oddkin/tokens/generate` and `oddkin-tokens build --format dtcg` (`<theme>.<mode>.tokens.json`): one complete Format Module 2025.10 document per context with explicit `$type`, typed values, curly-brace aliases, `$root`, and `$extensions["dev.oddkin"]`; a per-token lossless/lossy/unsupported mapping (default theme: 201 / 4 / 5; `em` letter spacing and `none` shadows unsupported and listed with their originals). Independent reader: `@terrazzo/parser` 2.10.0 (user-approved dev dependency) reads every document with `px`/`rem`-only dimensions and resolves every token, aliases included, to Oddkin's `resolveToken` value in four contexts. Byte-identical in Node, three engines, and a Worker; 10 of 10 mutants killed. Import and Resolver documents are out of scope (246, M9).)*
- [ ] DTCG Resolver 2025.10 experiment ([theming-and-styling.md](./docs/architecture/theming-and-styling.md) §9, R53): map defaults/preset/configuration/overrides, modes, products, density, profiles, conditions, and aliases onto sets, modifiers, contexts, and `resolutionOrder` through the canonical generator; test shared presets, independent overrides, preserved units, and 1/2/3/10 products; record determinism, permutation count, and output size; check with an independent Resolver implementation where practical; document supported, lossy, and unsupported mappings and precedence differences; decide export, import, both, a bounded subset, or an evidence-backed deferral. Format support never implies Resolver support. Owned by M9.
- [~] Text, focus, disabled/invalid/loading, and interaction colors have semantic contracts. Palette generation reports inaccessible role pairs and offers explicit corrections without silently changing a user's brand inputs. *(2026-10-21: `ROLE_CONTRACTS` in `@oddkin/tokens/generate` ([theming-and-styling.md](./docs/architecture/theming-and-styling.md) §10.4): text, interaction, focus, invalid, and status foregrounds against every surface components draw them on (WCAG 1.4.3 4.5:1, 1.4.11 3:1); disabled text measured and reported as exempt; loading reuses the enabled roles (`currentColor`). Generated roles are chosen against the same backgrounds, so 14 brand vectors alone and as products generate with no diagnostics. `contrast.fail` is one warning per role and mode naming every failing background, with a structured `correction` (closest passing reference step) that is never applied automatically; `contrastReport(theme)` measures every pair for the base theme and each product. 10 of 10 mutants killed. 2026-10-22: the default theme takes the owner's light `color.border.strong` `#868997` from the Figma variables snapshot (theming-and-styling.md §10.5), and its 3:1 contract covers every surface (lowest 3.03:1 on `surface.sunken`). Remaining: profiles that override colour roles are not yet checked.)*

Research: [DTCG Format 2025.10](https://www.designtokens.org/tr/2025.10/format/), [CSS variables](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-variables-1/), [CSS Color 4](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-color-4/), [Style Dictionary](https://styledictionary.com/), and [Tokens Studio](https://docs.tokens.studio/).

---

## 5a. Layout recipes and composition (summary)

Detailed design: [layout-recipes.md](./docs/architecture/layout-recipes.md).

- `defineLayoutRecipe({ container, regions, variants, when })` with `stack`, `row`, `grid`, `autoGrid`, `split`; module-scope evaluation; ordinary reusable CSS; no build transform; optional extraction emits identical CSS.
- Unbranded mechanism in `@oddkin/core/layout`; Oddkin defaults, conditions, presets, and page-author `layout.css` in `@oddkin/components/layout`.
- Cascade layers `recipe.base → variant → condition`; among normal author rules in the same encapsulation context (across stylesheets), unlayered declarations outrank layered ones. Shadow-root recipes stay encapsulated — consumers customize via role/token custom properties, component variables, exposed `::part`, and host styles; light-DOM recipes can be overridden by page unlayered rules. `!important` reverses layer precedence and has different cross-context ordering (layout-recipes.md §4). `:where()` flattening; `extend()` without mutation.
- Explicit container rules; intrinsic-first; no `ResizeObserver` for responsiveness. Component-author and page-author paths.
- [x] Document the exact container/wrapper/region contract: queries affect eligible descendants, containment changes sizing, and external selectors do not gain access to private shadow markup. Validate flat-tree/slot behavior, nested container selection/names, `::part`/`::slotted` limits, and inherited role variables in all baseline engines. *(layout-recipes.md §5; `packages/core/test/container-contract.browser.test.ts`, 8 contract checks in Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit, plus the existing recipe, role, and customization tests. Finding: container names are not encapsulated by shadow roots, so internal names are public; Oddkin recipes name only their host. Floor browsers are covered by the existing `container-query` probe.)*
- [x] Treat named thresholds as generated literal query inputs for the portable baseline. Do not assume ordinary runtime token overrides change compiled thresholds. Live condition editing must regenerate the relevant preview-owned query/recipe stylesheet without remounting or mutating shared static sheets. Any variable-based query syntax is progressive enhancement only after browser evidence. *(Literal thresholds and no runtime movement: `packages/components/test/conditions.browser.test.ts`. Live editing: `recipe.extend({ conditions })` re-resolves inherited conditions; `@oddkin/workbench/preview` `previewStyles` swaps in a preview-owned copy and replaces its rules; `packages/workbench/test/preview.browser.test.ts` in three engines shows a theme condition edit and an `odd-side-nav` threshold edit with the same nodes, focus, and expanded group, and the shared sheet and other instances untouched. No variable-based query syntax is used. 3 of 3 mutants killed.)*
- [ ] Page-author recipes expose useful CSS classes and CSS custom properties; component-author recipes stay ordinary readable CSS. Show handwritten equivalents and compare emitted rules and authoring time before adding template styling directives.

Research: [CSS container queries](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-conditional-5/#container-queries), [CSS Shadow](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-shadow-1/), and [cascade layers](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-cascade-5/).

## 5b. Data-driven composition (summary)

Detailed design: [data-composition.md](./docs/architecture/data-composition.md).

- Typed discriminated component models via JS properties; never mutated; intent events; native links; controlled **or** uncontrolled interaction state.
- Shared `@oddkin/core/collections` (keys, reconciliation, nesting, per-item `Scope`, state models); keyboard per APG pattern; no identity-only caching.
- One authoritative source per region; customization ladder (options → slots/parts → render functions → registered kinds); local vs portable configuration.
- `@oddkin/core/model` generates types, validators, JSON Schema, migrations, metadata. The composition schema (`@oddkin/components/composition`, [data-composition.md §8](./docs/architecture/data-composition.md)) describes an interface as data.
- [x] Clarify which types are inferred in TypeScript and which files are emitted. A single runtime definition supplies supported validators/schema/metadata; production components do not import schema emitters or a universal JSON renderer merely to accept typed objects. *(Types are inferred, never emitted: `Infer<typeof NavigationItemModel>` gives `NavigationItem`, and `@ts-expect-error` cases in `packages/components/test/models.test.ts` fail typecheck for invalid combinations. Emitted files: `scripts/build-component-schemas.mjs` writes `schemas/oddkin.navigation.v1.json` at build time (`--check` reports stale files), and `@oddkin/analyzer` publishes model metadata. The schema emitter `@oddkin/core/model/schema` is tooling only. `graph.test.ts` checks that the registration entry loads no model validators; `npm run size` fails if any measured graph loads `model/schema` (item 114). [data-composition.md](./docs/architecture/data-composition.md) §5, §7, §11.)*
- [x] Define stable keys, duplicate/missing-key diagnostics, large/deep input limits, locale-aware labels, and focus fallback when the focused item is removed. DOM identity preservation cannot preserve a node intentionally removed from the model. *(Keys and `collection.key.missing`/`.duplicate` with paths (`collections.test.ts`). Limits: a 1,000-level probe made `validate()` throw `RangeError`, and 10,000 levels did the same to `collection.set()`. Both now stop at `maxDepth` (default 32): `model.depth.exceeded` (also for cycles) and `collection.depth.exceeded`, each verified at 100,000 levels. 200,000 flat entries validate in under a second; `odd-side-nav` is tested at 1,000 entries and five levels. Labels are caller data with no built-in visible text (`odd-side-nav` robustness test), and the localization helpers are `@oddkin/core/i18n`. Focus fallback: `FocusRecovery` moves focus to the landmark when the focused entry is removed or hidden. The editable list hands focus to the row's other button at an edge, and Undo restores the row with a new scope. [data-composition.md](./docs/architecture/data-composition.md) §5, §11.)*
- [x] Plain object configuration remains sufficient for ordinary consumers. Builders are optional authoring aids. Serializable models cannot contain executable callbacks; local render functions, registered kinds, and services require explicit nonportable adapters. *(`odd-side-nav` takes plain `items` objects; no builder exists or is required (`examples/side-nav/`, the React and Vue fixtures). Model data cannot carry executable callbacks: a function where a label or action name belongs is `model.type.mismatch`, and an extra function property is `model.property.unknown` (warning), ignored, and never called (probe, 2026-10-19). `m.json` rejects functions (`model.test.ts`). `renderLabel` and `kinds` are property-only local customization, outside the model and its JSON Schema ([data-composition.md](./docs/architecture/data-composition.md) §4 "Local vs portable").)*
- [x] Keep native links and accessible component semantics intact in declarative, data-driven, and hybrid paths. Data configuration does not automatically choose a correct APG pattern. *(`side-nav.scopes.browser.test.ts`: the same links from data, slotted native links, and the hybrid (data plus header and footer slots) have the same names, targets, current state, and boxes in three engines. Data entries render native `<a href>` and `<button>`, and project kinds declare `semantics: 'link' | 'action'` explicitly to reuse those wrappers; `renderLabel` cannot remove them. The component picks the pattern: links use Tab order, and groups use the disclosure pattern ([data-composition.md](./docs/architecture/data-composition.md) §4, §12).)*

## 5c. Analyzer and metadata (summary)

Detailed design: [metadata-and-analyzer.md](./docs/architecture/metadata-and-analyzer.md).

- Optional, Node-only `@oddkin/analyzer` understands Oddkin authoring patterns (`OddkinElement.with`, `prop.*`, inheritance, re-exports, events, slots, parts, CSS properties, custom states, `define` entries, token references, recipes, models, JSDoc/TSDoc and Oddkin tags, preview roles, examples) using the **TypeScript compiler API** — no hand-written parser, no regex-first analysis, **never executes** component modules or expressions.
- Unsupported dynamic definitions → diagnostics + explicit static metadata.
- One normalized, versioned metadata model with stable ids and provenance (`extracted` / `authored` / `observed` / `verified`); unknown values stay unknown.
- Outputs: standard `custom-elements.json` (validated against a pinned CEM schema; `package.json` `customElements`), separately versioned `oddkin.metadata.json`, Workbench indexes and module maps, editor/schema outputs, agent bundles.
- Deterministic; watch + incremental; references validated against `exports`; public API comparison for releases.
- [ ] Separate source analysis from loading trusted Node configuration/build plugins. The no-execution guarantee applies to analyzed component modules and arbitrary source expressions; it must not imply that running an author's `.ts` config is a sandbox.
- [ ] Support published declarations and existing standard CEM as explicit input paths where source is unavailable; do not claim source inference that did not occur. Define merge precedence, contradictory metadata diagnostics, cache invalidation, and compatible schema migrations.
- [ ] Make analyzer output directly useful in editors: source positions, actionable diagnostic codes, custom data/schema exports, and correct installed-version links. A dedicated IDE language server is not required for v1.
- [ ] Share canonical metadata schemas as build inputs and emit browser-safe type/schema artifacts. Do not leak TypeScript compiler types, Node globals, or runtime imports from the analyzer into Workbench/browser declarations; record the owning contract entry before its first consumer ships.
- [ ] Evaluate a thin adapter/plugin for the existing CEM Analyzer ecosystem after the Oddkin vertical slice; reuse its interchange format without making that analyzer a runtime requirement or replacing the planned Oddkin-aware front end.

Research: [CEM schema repository](https://github.com/webcomponents/custom-elements-manifest), [existing analyzer and plugin model](https://custom-elements-manifest.open-wc.org/analyzer/getting-started/), and [TypeScript compiler API](https://github.com/microsoft/TypeScript/wiki/Using-the-Compiler-API).

## 5d. Workbench and Theme Studio (summary)

Detailed design: [workbench.md](./docs/architecture/workbench.md).

- Optional `@oddkin/workbench`: one reusable shell driven by metadata, config, component entry points, and optional examples. Full Oddkin experience, a defined standard-CEM baseline, and explicit "unknown/not documented" labels instead of invented behavior. Custom prefixes and branding. Presentation follows Design System Lab's structure with each library's own data (§5d.2).
- Component detail pages combine generated documentation with the interactive example and an **editable API table** (contract-correct controls: property vs attribute, `''`/`0`/`false`/`null`/unset, read-only, model editors, fixtures for non-serializable values); methods never auto-invoked; reset via clean instance; sync only through declared contracts; robust event log; reproducible plain HTML/JS snippets. No separate Playground destination.
- **Iframe-isolated previews** (own registry, module graph, styles, theme scopes, errors), owned by host and frame `Scope`s; independent viewport/container sizes; theme-value edits (incl. density, profiles, thresholds, presets, undo/redo, Reset theme) preserve instances; only component/example/composition/role-component changes, adapter/fixture/provider changes, registration replacement (fresh frame), or explicit Reset preview may replace the smallest affected region (workbench.md §5).
- **Theme Studio** as its own primary destination (`/theme-studio`) in every fully configured Workbench: same `ThemeConfig`, validation, and generator; form + JSON editors on one draft; strategy-aware controls; foundation/generated/alias/override labels; live, latest-wins regeneration preserving state and last valid preview; replaceable specimen recipe with light/dark panels; the library's own components mapped to specimen roles via declared capabilities or adapters (never tag-name guessing); token inspection and visualizations; comparison, undo/redo, presets, versioned sharing, canonical exports; static builds keep full client-side editing.
- `defineExamples` colocated, typed from metadata; reused by Workbench, docs, browser/visual tests, Theme Studio, agents.
- `oddkin analyze` · `oddkin workbench dev` · `oddkin workbench build`; never publishes or deploys; deterministic; authored files preserved.
- `apps/docs` embeds Workbench panels (foundations, component catalog/detail, Theme Studio); no duplicate foundation or component explorers or token engines.

### 5d.1 Workbench correctness, accessibility, and loading

- [x] Version and validate the shell/frame handshake, message source/origin, frame identity, request/generation IDs, and serializable payloads. Ignore messages from disposed or superseded frames. Incompatible library registrations require a fresh realm; HMR cannot unregister and redefine a custom element in place. *(Reviewed 2026-10-27 against `@oddkin/workbench/protocol`, `/preview`, and `/frame`: `FRAME_PROTOCOL_VERSION` in every envelope; the host accepts only `hello` from its own frame's window and origin, then talks over a `MessageChannel` port; both sides check frame id and generation, the frame refuses requests not newer than the last, every payload is checked as plain JSON with a shape validator per kind; superseded generations reject pending requests (`frame.generation.superseded`), a disposed frame rejects everything (`frame.disposed`), and an incompatible version never connects (`frame.version.incompatible`); a second registration of a tag fails with `frame.registration.incompatible` until `reload()` gives a fresh document, which changed modules also get in dev. `protocol.test.ts` and `frame.browser.test.ts` (4 tests × 3 engines, counted rejections by cause).)*
- [ ] Treat local component modules/examples as executable project code. Iframe isolation of registries/styles is not a promise of a security sandbox for arbitrary untrusted code. A user-code editor remains deferred.
- [ ] Lazy-load selected component modules, examples, detailed metadata, and heavy visualizations. Do not mount one iframe per catalog entry. Bound event logs, undo history, previews, and caches; coalesce slider edits while preserving a responsive input surface. Worker/latest-wins behavior must also measure wasted obsolete work, not just suppress stale commits.
- [ ] Test Workbench itself: landmarks/headings, skip links, keyboard navigation, accessible control names, error associations, restrained announcements, frame titles/focus entry and exit, numeric alternatives to sliders, textual swatch/state labels, reduced motion, forced colors, contrast, and narrow layouts. Generated docs accessibility is part of the product's definition of done.
- [ ] Unknown or unavailable controls remain explained and discoverable; unsupported adapters/providers do not produce misleading empty success previews. Implement the remount policy table in workbench.md §5: theme-value edits and compatible CSS/token/doc/metadata HMR update in place; component code HMR replacing a registration uses a fresh frame. Never derive component keys or frame identity from a theme configuration hash. Instance preservation does not freeze internal DOM; preserve state, focus, and selection where the elements remain present.
- [x] `defineExamples` has one plain serializable description layer plus explicit owned fixture hooks. Keep generic example/config helpers importable and testable without mounting Workbench. Resolve the build as runtime components → analyzer metadata → example typechecks/tests → Workbench shell, with helper entries available first; no production dependency from components to tooling. *(ADR 0021 Accepted: colocated `*.examples.ts` are development-only sources — excluded from the package `tsconfig.json`, so never built or shipped; type-checked through the tests program; `@oddkin/workbench` is a components `devDependency` only. The boundary tool's `devSources` rule lets them import `@oddkin/workbench`, rejects shipped modules importing them (`boundary.import.devSource`), and fails on any example file in `dist` (`boundary.artifact.devSource`). `mountExample` in `@oddkin/workbench/testing` runs an example and its fixture in browser tests without the shell.)*
- [ ] Theme Studio's complete interactive capability is available in static builds; initial gallery samples, request logs, and share settings use deterministic fixtures and explicit versioning. Export pending/error states are visible and never silently export an older configuration as the latest draft.

Research: [Storybook Web Components](https://storybook.js.org/docs/get-started/frameworks/web-components-vite), [WCAG 2.2](https://www.w3.org/TR/WCAG22/), [APG](https://www.w3.org/WAI/ARIA/apg/), and [HTML cross-document messaging](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/web-messaging.html#crossDocumentMessages).

### 5d.2 Information architecture and presentation (Design System Lab structure, project data)

Detailed design: [workbench-pages.md](./docs/architecture/workbench-pages.md). Reference: the rendered audit of Design System Lab from 2026-10-17 (document removed in dbb6f2d; findings in M0).

- [ ] **Page registry** is the single source for routes, primary navigation, contextual sidebars, overview cards, search entries, visibility, and legacy redirects; stable page/section ids shared with metadata and authored content.
- [ ] **Shell** reproduces the reference's presentation with the library's branding: persistent primary navigation, contextual left sidebar (grouped where applicable; collapsible on narrow screens), eyebrow + title + summary headers, back links, "On this page" table of contents, restrained surfaces, card overviews (3/2/1 columns by container width). Shell appearance and preview appearance are separate.
- [ ] **Primary navigation**: Foundations · Components · Theme Studio · Products and modes (only when enabled; configurable placement) · guides and other registry sections. No separate Playground destination.
- [ ] **Foundations overview**: cards for Token architecture, Color, Typography, Spacing, Borders and radii, Elevation, Motion, Themes, Responsive behavior — each only when supported; title, summary, `Updated · tags` from metadata and authored overrides; dates only from declared metadata or source history, never build time; no specimens in cards; Theme Studio never appears as a card or sidebar entry.
- [ ] **Foundation detail pages** generated from the library's configuration and metadata (table in workbench-pages.md §5.2), with Spacing as the reference vertical slice: scale table (token, CSS custom property, source-unit value, proportional sample), semantic vocabulary and precedence only where declared, derived counts/ranges, computed pixels only with an explicit preview context. Pages show the saved/generated baseline unless a Theme Studio draft is explicitly selected and labelled; never mixed.
- [ ] **Components catalog**: grouped sidebar, search (name, summary, tags), configurable category filter with fallback group, result counts, grouped cards, URL filter state, no previews in cards, evidence-backed claims only.
- [ ] **Component detail page** combines Overview · Interactive example (preview, presets, Reset, Copy example link, preview settings, generated plain HTML/JS) · API (editable table: Name, Type, Default, Current value, Description, attribute/reflection) · slots, events, methods, parts, CSS properties, custom states · Examples · Tokens (property → CSS property → token trace; every token read with tier, read-through, and context; declared/potential/observed labels) · Behavior and accessibility · Guidance · Source · Known limitations. Preview stays observable while editing (adjacent/sticky on wide screens; stacked on narrow).
- [ ] **Products and modes** disabled and absent by default; explicit product definitions from local theme config, installed packages, or versioned manifests (snapshotted at build time); workspace discovery only suggests.
- [ ] **Content layers**: built-in reusable copy, generated facts (authoritative for values and contracts), and authored overrides keyed by stable ids (typed `oddkin.docs.ts` / Markdown front matter) that survive regeneration, with validation, precedence, localization, and migration rules.

---

## 5e. AI-friendly architecture and generated agent kits (summary)

Detailed design: [agent-tooling.md](./docs/architecture/agent-tooling.md); decision: [ADR 0032](./docs/architecture/decisions/0032-generated-agent-kits.md) (extends ADR 0022).

**Central outcome:** a team adopting Oddkin can generate accurate, version-aware agent support for its own library from the same authoritative inputs used by its documentation and Workbench — component metadata, configuration, authored guidance, and examples. The kit covers that team's own packages and exports, prefix and component APIs, tokens and theme axes (themes, modes, density, profiles), recipes and conditions, examples and composition patterns, design guidance, repository conventions, and verification commands, and must help an assistant that starts with no knowledge of Oddkin or the library.

- Optional, dev-only `@oddkin/agent` over analyzer outputs: project context, kit generation, instructions, operations (inspect project, find/inspect components, tokens, recipes, explain/validate config, validate usage, scaffold, migrations, data validation, composition advice, preview-role mapping, export verification, preview inspection, run checks), CLI and optional MCP adapter as separate layers. Uses the developer's IDE agent/model; Oddkin supplies contracts, deterministic operations, and evidence.
- **One metadata model.** Agent-facing facts are views of the normalized model (CEM for interoperability, Oddkin enrichment kept separate), referencing existing property, event, model, and composition declarations by stable id. Provenance stays *extracted* / *authored* / *observed* / *verified* (with conditions); unresolved information is explicit; nothing is inferred from names or types; the no-execution guarantee holds.
- **Project- and version-aware.** Installed versions, exports, registrations, project components, token configuration, recipes, examples, conventions, and commands are resolved locally; every artifact and result carries a fingerprint and freshness state; stale artifacts are detected and never silently replaced by newer website docs. Capability paths (core-only, tokens-only, components without state, full application) are respected.
- **Generated kit.** A small entry guide plus focused references loaded on demand (install/consume, choosing components, authoring, theming, layout, data models, lifecycle and `Scope`, frameworks, debugging, migration), in Markdown and JSON. Exact API references are generated; authored design guidance is checked for valid API references at build time. Optional packaging: Agent Skills, a managed `AGENTS.md` section that preserves existing text, IDE adapters, and clean Markdown + `llms.txt` for static docs (client loading behavior documented, not assumed). Independent of building or publishing Workbench.
- **Examples as shared evidence**, including explained incorrect examples for common mistakes; compiling, static validity, and browser verification stay distinct.
- **Repairable diagnostics** through an optional envelope over the existing diagnostic shapes, and **preview feedback** through Workbench frames that execute only trusted project examples.
- Reports provenance; schema validity is never presented as proof of accessibility, behavior, or design quality. AI support stays optional: ordinary component consumers need no agent tooling, Workbench, Oddkin state, application services, or hosted AI service; no mandatory embedding service, hosted model, or second analyzer.
- Improved AI usability is a hypothesis verified by the evaluation in agent-tooling.md §11.
- **Revised 2026-10-26 ([ADR 0046](./docs/architecture/decisions/0046-plan-audit-2026-10-26.md)):** every published package ships its version-matched kit (`agent/` and `llms.txt`, generated at build and checked by `verify-packs`; D6); `@oddkin/agent`'s read-only operations start now as a CLI and an MCP server on the official MCP TypeScript SDK, used by the implementing agents (D7); Figma Code Connect template files are generated from the catalog so design-to-code agents receive Oddkin markup (D8); the composition schema is the agent-facing UI description (D3).

Research: R33–R41 in [synthesis.md](./docs/research/synthesis.md) and §12.4.

---

## 5f. Product documentation, localization, and maintainability

- [ ] Give each adoption path one recommended starting point, an honest package/feature matrix, copyable JS/TS examples, and a direct route from an error code to the relevant guide. Optional features should not require understanding the entire system.
- [ ] Keep Oddkin architecture/decision case studies visible: problem, constraints, alternatives, selected API, working demonstration, measurements, and limitations. Separate measured results from design hypotheses; adapt the original application's design-engineering discussions into product content.
- [ ] Define localization contracts for visible component strings, accessible names, validation/status messages, inherited `lang`/`dir`, logical layout, and browser `Intl` formatting. Keep consumer-provided labels first-class and avoid coupling localization to stores/router. A translation platform is not required.
- [ ] Plan static, readable documentation content and page metadata, stable canonical links, local search indexes, and version labels. Decide which API/guide content should be readable/indexable before client boot through build-time document generation; this does not imply an Oddkin SSR/hydration renderer. Keep deferred SSR/hydration limits explicit in framework guides.
- [ ] Define loading/failure/fallback presentation for unregistered elements, lazy routes, fonts/assets, and unavailable modules. Do not hide an entire application until every custom element is defined or claim zero-JavaScript interactive custom elements.
- [ ] Include an optional `oddkin doctor` operation in tooling for installed versions, exports, config/schema compatibility, registrations, and supported setup checks. Existing focused commands remain usable without installing Workbench; any unified CLI delegates to the same implementations rather than duplicating them.
- [ ] Package optional agent instructions/plugin assets alongside installed-version metadata and deterministic operations. Support the developer's existing IDE agent; no hosted model service is required. Evaluate generated source changes and migration patches against the same checks as handwritten work.
- [ ] Review scope at every milestone: remove or defer helpers with no demonstrated reuse, keep public APIs smaller than internals, and extract behavior controllers only from working components. Do not add SSR, an editor platform, a page builder, or a broad server framework to complete this plan.

Research: [Intl / ECMA-402](https://tc39.es/ecma402/), [HTML language and direction](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/dom.html#the-lang-and-xml:lang-attributes), [MCP specification](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/latest), and the framework-integration sources in §12.2.

---

## 5g. Multi-product design systems (summary)

Detailed design: [multi-product.md](./docs/architecture/multi-product.md); decision: [ADR 0033](./docs/architecture/decisions/0033-multi-product-foundations.md) (extends ADR 0026).

**Contract:** Oddkin supports shared interaction foundations, product-specific visual implementations, and scoped token themes. Teams reuse supported behavior and templates through public exports; product implementations keep documented interaction contracts and pass shared verification fixtures.

- **Identity.** Product identity is independent of repository, component family, design language, theme, mode, density, and profile. Two products may share a design language; one product may support several themes. Design languages map onto existing theme scopes and product values onto existing product scopes; no new runtime provider or CSS scope axis is mandatory.
- **Any number of products.** One, two, three, ten, or more products use one model keyed by stable ids: no hardcoded counts, fixed comparison slots, or product-specific schema fields. A single product needs no product configuration or selection ceremony. Shared presets, token contracts, families, and foundations are referenced, not duplicated; products override only what differs; changing one product never mutates shared presets or another product's resolved output. Products may live in one repository, several workspaces, separate repositories, or installed packages. Two-product fixtures are representative, not a limit.
- **Layers.** Recomposable components export behavior, template (anatomy), documented structural styles, default visual styles, and a composed definition; a `foundation` entry loads no default skin. Oddkin's own components are built from the same public pieces. Import graphs are documented separately from installation dependencies.
- **Recomposition.** `compose()` reuses an existing definition to create a new frozen definition with its own identity and a traceable foundation link, without mutating the source or copying registration state; elements resolve configuration by exact constructor. One style-ordering policy; sheets stay shared per document. Existing registration rules hold (`registry.collision`, `registry.constructorReused`) plus `registry.definitionConflict`; scoped registries are never assumed.
- **Customization ladder:** theme values → component variables/parts/slots/options → product styles composed with structural styles → alternative template satisfying an anatomy contract → reused or extended behavior. Levels 1–2 update instances in place; levels 3–5 select a different implementation and follow the structural replacement rules.
- **Anatomy contracts** for components that support alternative templates; alternative templates and styles are verified against shared interaction fixtures, and foundation verification never certifies downstream skins.
- **Component families:** a composite renders the product's children through one explicit, analyzable mechanism connecting named roles, selected definitions, rendered references, registration, and compatibility — chosen by prototype, never tag guessing or global substitution.
- **Distribution and scopes:** explicit package/config/manifest references; foundation compatibility ranges; scope behavior specified for Shadow DOM, slotted content, overlays, relocation, and adoption; resets and fonts opt-in; CSS growth measured, not emitted for every combination.
- **Tooling:** metadata records foundations, derived definitions, layered sources, families, anatomy, and compatibility; Workbench compares chosen products with searchable selection and on-demand loading (Products and modes stays optional and absent by default); agent lookups are scoped to a product and generated on demand.

Research: R26, R42–R46 in [synthesis.md](./docs/research/synthesis.md) and §12.1.

---

## 5h. Change impact, conformance, explanation, reproduction, and page lifecycle (summary)

Detailed design: [change-impact-and-verification.md](./docs/architecture/change-impact-and-verification.md) (ADR 0034) and [core-contracts.md](./docs/architecture/core-contracts.md) §9.1 (ADR 0035).

These build on the composition API and folders (§3.3), agent kits (§5e), and multi-product foundations (§5g) as integration constraints, and reuse existing sources through typed relationships: component definitions and public contracts, analyzer metadata, the canonical token generator and its graph, recipes and conditions, shared examples and fixtures, reactive and `Scope` records, Workbench and Theme Studio, and optional CLI/agent operations. No second analyzer, token engine, reactive engine, registry, or documentation catalog. Extracted, authored, observed, and verified facts stay distinguishable.

- **Change impact** (`@oddkin/analyzer`): versioned comparison snapshots over existing artifacts; stable-id matching; renames only from authoritative identity or migration records; incompatible / review / informational classes with no automatic semver mapping for token values; traversal with edge provenance across components, derived definitions, tokens, recipes, products, families, examples, and suites; unresolved edges widen verification; conservative fallbacks; verification states `selected`, `passed`, `failed`, `reused`, `not-executed`, `unresolved`; local `impact-report.v1` for CLI, Workbench, agents, and CI; explicit visual-baseline accept.
- **Portable conformance suites** (`@oddkin/workbench/conformance`, `./testing`): versioned contracts, adapters over public capabilities and documented anatomy, author-written assertions, invariants bound to declared change kinds (respecting structural replacement), mutants that must fail and customizations that must pass, representative coverage of products (1/2/3/10) and environments, evidence per implementation/configuration/browser/suite version; never accessibility certification; never in ordinary component imports.
- **Explanations** (`explanation.v1`, one model for Workbench, CLI, and agents): styling chains from observed values through component variables, roles, aliases, scopes, overrides, and recipes, with limitations; responsive conditions active only when reliably observed; measurement only in explicit inspection; runtime records (tracking path, dependencies, commits, owning scope, active/cancelled/superseded/suspended/disposed) from a development-only, opt-in, bounded `@oddkin/core/inspect` absent from production graphs.
- **Runnable reproductions** (`reproduction.v1`): distinct from share links; only declared inputs; undeclared services/state listed with required adapters; deterministic fixtures, not arbitrary replay; public exports and normal resolution; verified in clean directories; never published.
- **DTCG Resolver**: explicit experiment and decision (§5.1); Format support never implies Resolver support.
- **Page suspension and restoration**: five distinct lifecycles; no disposal on `pagehide`; explicit owned app service; declared service and resource restore policies; real persisted-restoration evidence only.
- **Priority:** prototype portable interaction verification, then change-impact reporting, on `odd-button` and customized implementations before generalizing; explanation and reproduction follow from the proven contracts. Reuse existing runners, browser automation, screenshots, and diagnostics; build no new runner, automation framework, screenshot engine, or hosted service. Each prototype records task, inputs, authority, unknowns, acceptance, cost, owner, and expand/revise/defer condition (change-impact-and-verification.md §6).

Research: R26, R29, R30, R33, R47–R55 in [synthesis.md](./docs/research/synthesis.md) and §12.

## 5i. CSS architecture, compatibility, and progressive enhancement (summary)

Detailed design: [css-architecture.md](./docs/architecture/css-architecture.md) (ADR 0009 floor, ADR 0038 compatibility policy, ADR 0039 layer order); registry [features.json](./docs/compatibility/features.json); generated [feature matrix](./docs/compatibility/matrix.md).

A reliable CSS baseline at a pinned floor, with modern capabilities added as tested enhancements through the existing canonical infrastructure — one token engine, one style-delivery path, one cascade-layer order — and no second styling system, transpiler, or polyfill.

- **Support policy (CSS-01):** **Baseline 2024 ([ADR 0046](./docs/architecture/decisions/0046-plan-audit-2026-10-26.md) D1), re-resolved on 2026-10-26:** Chrome/Edge 130, Firefox 132, Safari 18.2, and their mobile equivalents, the versions that support every feature newly available by 2024-12-31 (`node scripts/compat/baseline-floor.mjs`), with `@mdn/browser-compat-data` 8.1.6 and `web-features` 3.43.0 (exact dev dependencies); the floor is the Baseline year that is Widely Available at or near the v1 release (on 2026-10-26: Chrome/Edge 123, Firefox 124, Safari 17.4). Previously Chrome/Edge 125, Firefox 126, Safari 17.4 (resolved 2026-10-17). Categories: foundation · enhancement · experimental · deferred/declined; browsers below the floor are unsupported. The JavaScript floor matches (ES2024 target and library; DOM features above the floor feature-detected).
- **Feature and fallback matrix (CSS-02):** every adopted or evaluated feature with each BCD subfeature relied on, gate, baseline and enhanced behavior, expected differences, floor exceptions (popover light dismiss on Safari iOS 17.0–18.2; `cursor` and `resize` on mobile), and cited evidence. `npm run lint:css` validates the registry against the pinned data and lints every shipped and example stylesheet: above-floor features must be registered enhancements behind `@supports` or a literal parse-time fallback, never a `var()` fallback, never in a shared selector list.
- **Tokens and colour (CSS-03, CSS-04):** one engine; generated CSS layered in `odd.tokens`; values within the floor (`value.syntax.aboveFloor`), because a defined custom property holding unparseable syntax invalidates its readers at computed-value time; enhanced token values only from generator-emitted `@supports` blocks per scope; alias closure keeps nested themes correct; literal query thresholds. Colour: sRGB hex output; P3 deferred to M9 behind `(color-gamut: p3)` after contrast verification; `color-mix()` for M9 scrims and elevation; relative colours deferred; `light-dark()` for colour tokens re-opened and decided by measurement ([ADR 0046](./docs/architecture/decisions/0046-plan-audit-2026-10-26.md) D2; declined earlier because Safari 17.4 lacked it); `contrast-color()` declined as an accessibility mechanism.
- **Typed custom properties (CSS-05):** no registrations; tokens, component variables, and mode toggles are never registered (a registered initial value masks `var()` fallbacks — shown in Chrome 125 — and Firefox 126 lacks `@property`); component-private registrations from M10 under the written policy.
- **Cascade and customization (CSS-06):** `@layer odd.reset, odd.tokens, odd.base, odd.layout, odd.components, odd.utilities;` with application overrides unlayered; documented precedence across layers, `!important`, and shadow boundaries; theme precedence component overrides → application configuration → scoped configuration → system preferences → library defaults; customization through tokens, component variables, parts, custom states, and attributes; `@scope` only for docs styling (M11).
- **Layout (CSS-07):** contracts of the existing helpers recorded; constrained content, media frames, scroll regions, and subgrid from demonstrated needs; primitives never declare containers, compositions own and name them; queries add transitions and never gate essential function.
- **Overlays, controls, motion (CSS-08–CSS-10):** planned with the M10 components — one shared Oddkin positioning controller, native anchoring only when every needed subfeature is detected, exactly one backend at a time, top layer instead of portals; controller-derived custom states (implemented for `odd-input`); `field-sizing` and customizable `<select>` as gated enhancements over native baselines; no cleanup that waits on animation events.
- **Typography and input modes (CSS-11):** implemented for current components (`rem` sizing, forced-colors system colours, outline focus, hover gating, long-content wrapping, 320 px reflow); fluid scales, font metrics, `text-wrap`, and text-box trimming planned (M9, M10).
- **Delivery and performance (CSS-12):** shared constructed sheets, strict CSP, CSS gzip budgets in `npm run size`; containment and `content-visibility` only after measurement (M8).
- **Compatibility build (CSS-13):** one output with progressive enhancements; no transpiler or alternate distribution; the generator performs the only static value conversion (OKLCH → sRGB hex).
- **Experiments (CSS-14):** `if()`, `@function`, typed `attr()`, container style and scroll-state queries, `corner-shape`, scoped view transitions, `interpolate-size`, text-box trimming — isolated, each with use case, support, benefit, fallback, cost, and decision.
- **Verification (CSS-15):** regression scenarios F01–F16 (§7 criteria 81–96), the CSS gallery, and the floor probe in Chrome for Testing 125 and Firefox 126; current Playwright engines are not evidence for historical releases.

- [x] Resolve and pin the support policy, record data versions and the Baseline comparison, and align the JavaScript floor (CSS-01) *(ADR 0009 Accepted; css-architecture.md §2)*
- [x] Registry, schema, lint of shipped and example CSS, generated matrix, and lint unit tests in `npm run verify` (CSS-02, CSS-15) *(see M4 CSS compatibility slice)*
- [ ] Re-resolve the floor and data for each release and publish the matrix with release notes (M12)
- [ ] Close the evidence gaps: Safari 17.4 and Safari iOS 17.4 runtime evidence on a macOS host, the floor run in CI on linux64, mobile floor browsers (M8, M12)
- [ ] Planned contracts: tokens (M9), overlays, controls, motion, layout primitives, typed private properties (M10), docs presentation and `@scope` (M11) — tracked in those milestones

Research: §12.3 "CSS compatibility and progressive enhancement".

## 5j. AI interaction package `@oddkin/ai` (summary)

Detailed design: [ai-interaction.md](./docs/architecture/ai-interaction.md) (ADR 0040). Source requirement: AI-01–AI-13 with patterns C01–C20 and acceptance scenarios A01–A22 (prompt preserved in `docs/AI_COMPONENTS_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`). Package guide: [packages/ai/README.md](./packages/ai/README.md).

An optional browser package, parallel to the milestone track (it needs only M3 core and the M4 composition API, `odd-button`, and analyzer support), for conversation interfaces, inline assistance, research tools, and agent workflows. Elements render host-owned records and request operations through typed events; DOM-free controllers and adapters own lifecycles; the host owns data, transport, policy, and persistence. Element names use `odd-` (ADR 0037) in place of the specification's `ok-`.

- **Boundaries and ownership (AI-02):** depends on core, tokens (refs), components (`odd-button`); no state/app/tooling/framework/third-party runtime; one authoritative source per surface (controller or application store); presentation state only in elements.
- **Records (AI-04):** 14 `@oddkin/core/model` definitions (message and parts, tool call, approval, artifact, usage, checkpoint, plan, question, context item, model option, feedback, suggestion, transcript segment, conversation snapshot) with generated JSON Schemas; core gains `m.json()`.
- **Lifecycles (AI-05):** separate attempt, connection, stop, tool, and approval machines with transition tables; capabilities with reasons; stop ≠ disconnect; approval ≠ success; stale and late responses ignored.
- **Adapters (AI-06):** simulation, external store, AI SDK UI message stream v1 (recorded fixtures; inspected 2026-10-17); WHATWG SSE parser; identity-only deduplication; AG-UI design note and A2UI evaluation.
- **Elements (AI-03, AI-07):** 24 `odd-*` elements; properties for records, attributes for scalars; typed bubbling composed events; React 19 and Vue 3.5 fixtures.
- **Input, scrolling, resources (AI-08), rendering (AI-09), accessibility (AI-10):** IME-safe composer with draft revisions; follow-latest scrolling with anchor preservation; UTF-16 ranges bound to revisions; streaming Markdown by DOM construction with a URL allow-list; one announcer per surface; localized `strings`.

| A21 Keyboard/screen-reader flows without chatter or focus theft | [~] | `conversation.browser.test.ts`, `conversation.test.ts` (announcements once, never streamed text; focus kept); axe in e2e. Real screen-reader output not verified |
| A22 Three static demos from a production build | [x] | `e2e/ai-demos.spec.ts` (Chromium, Firefox, WebKit; strict CSP and Trusted Types) |

- [x] Package, records, controllers, adapters, 24 elements, framework bindings, metadata, schemas, demos, docs, ADR 0040 *(verification.md 2026-10-18 entry)*
- [x] Performance fixture (1,000 messages, sustained streaming, DOM and heap) and measured size budgets *(verification.md)*
- [ ] Screen-reader verification (NVDA + Firefox, VoiceOver + Safari) of the conversation feed, announcements, approvals, and inline assist; manual forced-colors and 200 %/400 % zoom review
- [x] Machine-readable tested examples and invalid-usage guidance in metadata (with the M4/M5 `defineExamples` decision) *(`packages/ai/src/**/*.examples.ts`: 43 examples, at least a default for each of the 24 elements, through `defineExamples` (ADR 0021) and public `@oddkin/*` entries only. `packages/ai/test/examples.browser.test.ts` mounts every correct example in three engines; each reports nothing, renders, and releases everything it owned. Four explained mistakes, one per usage diagnostic: `odd-sources` `sources` attribute, `odd-prompt-input` `disabled="false"` and a `toolbar` slot, `odd-artifact` `mode="code"`. Each shows its stated consequence against its correct example, and only `mode="code"` is reported at runtime. `packages/ai/test/examples.test.ts` checks examples against the published CEM, pairs each mistake with the diagnostic `validateUsage` gives it, validates `examples.v1`, and compares the metadata listing with the analyzer's static read. Making the slot mistake correct fails both suites.)*
- [ ] AG-UI adapter with recorded fixtures (only if requested; design note in ai-interaction.md §5.5)
