## 6. Milestones

| # | Milestone | Depends on |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M0 | Reconcile research, reference inventory (incl. Theme Studio reference), ADRs, metadata contracts | — |
| M1 | Workspace, distribution, enforced boundaries (incl. tooling packages) | M0 ADRs |
| M2 | Token vertical slice: config → CSS → CSS-only consumer; roles, conditions, profiles | M1 |
| M3 | Core: reactivity, `Scope`, element, templates, styles, recipes, collections, models, registry; **analyzer prototype + metadata model v1 draft**; first measurements | M1 |
| M4 | Button, input, **card**, side-nav (recipe + data), editable list; analyzer emits validated CEM + enrichment; interoperable consumers | M2, M3 |
| M5 | **Workbench vertical slice** (docs, controls, isolated previews, event log, snippets, first Theme Studio) on the four components + docs app slice | M4 |
| M6 | Stores, context, persistence/inspection, app root | M3 |
| M7 | Resources, router, async recipe; docs and Workbench on the router; static builds under a subpath | M5, M6 |
| M8 | Correctness, accessibility, performance, ergonomics → **v1 compatibility checkpoint** | M4–M7 |
| M9 | Complete token domains/outputs + **complete Theme Studio** | M2, M5, M8 |
| M10 | Component catalog + behavior controllers; examples and metadata per component | M8, relevant M9 domains |
| M11 | Docs IA, guides, Workbench completion, migrations, onboarding, **`@oddkin/agent`** | M7, M9, M10 |
| M12 | Packed consumers, release verification, deployment readiness | all |

M2 and M3 run in parallel after M1. M6 can run alongside M4–M5. Benchmark fixtures start in M3. Metadata contracts are defined in M0, prototyped by the analyzer in M3, validated on four real components in M4–M5, and receive an initial compatibility checkpoint at M8. Additive M9–M11 capabilities must be validated before the final release freeze at M12; material schema changes require an explicit version/migration decision. Agent-kit metadata and diagnostic contracts are drafted with M4's metadata validation; local project context and generated references are piloted on the M4–M5 slice; agent-facing contracts are reviewed at M8; `@oddkin/agent` operations, packaging, and the MCP adapter can start once analyzer outputs exist (after M5) but ship in M11; packed verification and the evaluation complete before the M12 freeze. Conformance and impact prototypes come first (M4, M5), with evidence reviewed at M8; explanation and reproduction expand in M9–M11; page-lifecycle policy follows the app services in M7. Multi-product customization levels are prototyped on M4 components before catalog expansion, reviewed at M8, and completed for tokens and Workbench in M9 and per component in M10. **Do not expand the catalog, recipe presets, data composition, or Workbench features beyond the vertical slices before M8 resolves foundation problems.** Owner decision (2026-10-21): building the components of the Figma Common primitives and aligning the AI components starts now, in parallel with the M8 checkpoint (component-catalog.md; data grid still deferred). The optional AI interaction track (§5j, `@oddkin/ai`) runs in parallel: it depends only on M3 core, the M4 composition API and `odd-button`, and analyzer metadata; its contracts are reviewed with the others at M8 and its packed verification joins M12. **Priority after the 2026-10-26 audit ([ADR 0046](./docs/architecture/decisions/0046-plan-audit-2026-10-26.md)):** finish the round's catalog families; then the Baseline 2024 floor (D1) and the `light-dark()` measurement (D2); the core semantics of §3.1–§3.2 (D5) and the comparative benchmarks (D4) before the catalog grows further; then the composition schema (D3), version-matched kits and early `@oddkin/agent` (D6, D7), Code Connect (D8), and the autoloader (D9).

### M4 — First interoperable components and validated metadata

- [x] `@oddkin/tokens/refs` and `@oddkin/tokens/values` — DOM-free, registration-free
**Composition API and component folders** (§3.3; build before the four components so they are born in the new structure)

- [x] Design and record (ADR) the public composition API: `compose({ name, element, template, styles, dependencies? })` returning a definition, and `define(definition)` alongside the existing `define(tagName, ctor)`. Requirements: an external template runs in the same tracked render context as `render()`, so the binding rule, lifecycle order, `Scope` cleanup, and DOM identity are unchanged; templates are typed from a type-only host import; definitions are side-effect free and statically analyzable; the counter, component, and list size budgets (ADR 0031) still pass. The name clash is resolved: `compose()` (`@oddkin/core/element`) is the definition composer; the optional data-composition schema renderer becomes `renderComposition()` from `@oddkin/components/composition`. *([ADR 0036](./docs/architecture/decisions/0036-component-composition-api.md); `packages/core/src/element/definition.ts`; `packages/core/test/definition.browser.test.ts`, 17 tests in three engines; counter 9,521 B of 9,728 B)*
- [x ] Analyzer: follow `compose()` definitions and external template and style modules (slots, parts, CSS properties, token reads) and recognize `define(definition)` registrations; fixtures cover both the folder structure and colocated authoring. *(`tests/fixtures/analyzer-composed` vs `analyzer-library`: identical CEM declarations; `definition` metadata with `extends`, template, styles, dependencies; `@internal` excluded; dynamic cases diagnosed)*
- [x ] Boundary check: no ordinary barrel or `index.ts` reaches a `*.define.ts` module; registration entries are listed as side-effectful in package metadata; each component folder's `index.ts` imports without registering anything. *(`boundary.registration.barrel`: transitive walk from every `index.*` in package and example sources, content-aware for `define.*`/`define/` so the `define()` implementation is not flagged; `boundary.registration.sideEffects`: registration entries must be listed in `"sideEffects"`; 5 checker tests; deliberate violation in `examples/core-counter/src/index.ts` caught and reverted)*
- [x ] **Migrate existing component work** to the folder structure. Current inventory (2026-10-17; nothing moved yet): *(Done 2026-10-17: `examples/core-counter` migrated — class, template, styles, definition, define entry, side-effect-free `index.ts`, `src/define.ts`; `tests/fixtures/analyzer-composed` added beside the colocated fixture; dynamic fixture, `examples/binding-rule`, and inline fixtures preserved; no `@oddkin/components` components existed. Old per-file modules `dist/demo-*.js` were not kept: the example has no package exports and its consumers use `dist/index.js`, which keeps every export but no longer registers — the intended change, with registration moved to `dist/define.js`.)*
  - `examples/core-counter` (`demo-counter`, `demo-greeting`) — reference examples that teach component authoring: **migrate**. Its `src/index.ts` registers on import today, so its registration moves to explicit define entries.
  - `tests/fixtures/analyzer-library` (`acme-panel`, `acme-alert`) — analyzer fixtures: **add** a folder-structured counterpart; keep the colocated fixture so both paths stay covered.
  - `tests/fixtures/analyzer-dynamic` (`dynamic-element`) — deliberately dynamic definition for diagnostics: **preserve**.
  - `examples/binding-rule` (plain-JavaScript and TypeScript walkthrough elements) — intentionally colocated demonstration: **preserve**.
  - Focused inline fixtures in `packages/*/test`, `research/benchmarks`, and `research/experiments`: **preserve**.
  - `@oddkin/components`: no components yet; `odd-button`, `odd-input`, `odd-card`, and `odd-side-nav` are created in the new structure.

  For each migrated component:
  1. Inventory its current class, template, styles, registration, exports, and supporting files.
  2. Move its implementation into the component folder structure.
  3. Extract its template and styles without changing behavior.
  4. Assemble its definition through `compose()`.
  5. Keep registration in the explicit registration entry.
  6. Update imports, package exports, examples, documentation, analyzer fixtures, and source references.
  7. Preserve existing public import paths through compatible re-exports where practical.
  8. Verify that no ordinary barrel accidentally imports a registration entry.

Existing evidence (e.g. `e2e/core-counter.spec.ts`, its CEM fixture, and its strict-CSP check) must keep passing unchanged or be updated with a recorded reason. *(core-counter: CEM declarations identical apart from module paths and one corrected description; `e2e/core-counter.spec.ts` unchanged and passing under strict CSP in three engines; `tests/examples/core-counter-migration.browser.test.ts` — barrel registers nothing, explicit define registers, click writes only inside `<output>` with stable nodes, tracked label update, events/states/Escape, reflection and property-only arrays, scope counts at baseline after 10 reconnects; emitted declarations type-checked by `tests/examples/core-counter.types.test.ts`; analyzer fixtures regenerated (module paths, `registration` now the define entry, new `definition` field). **Foundation completion** (2026-10-18; before further components, so new components are born on shared primitives)

`odd-button`, `odd-input`, `odd-card`, and the 24 `@oddkin/ai` elements each re-implement host defaults, the hidden-attribute guarantee, focus rings, visually hidden text, roving focus, focus recovery, live announcements, observers, and localized strings. These are now demonstrated shared needs (§5f "extract behavior controllers only from working components"), so they are extracted before the catalog grows. Core stays unbranded (no `--odd-*`, no Oddkin event names); token-bound primitives live in `@oddkin/components`. Production components stay as lean as before: primitives are shared constructed sheets and tree-shakable functions, and existing size budgets must still pass.

- [x] Opt-in document stylesheets generated from the same modules: `@oddkin/components/css/reset.css` (`odd.reset`), `base.css` (`odd.base`), `utilities.css` (`odd.utilities`), each preceded by the canonical layer order (ADR 0039) and linted by `npm run lint:css` *(`packages/components/src/css/index.ts` → `scripts/build-component-css.mjs`; layer order, no `!important`, utilities and base share the primitives' declarations (`styles.test.ts`); linted (50 stylesheets); budget 768 B, measured 612 B)*

- [x] Analyzer runs in the components build; publishes `custom-elements.json` (+ `customElements` field) and `oddkin.metadata.json` *(`scripts/build-metadata.mjs` in `npm run build`, strict — any analyzer diagnostic fails the build; `packages/components/test/metadata.test.ts`; packed tarball contains both files and `verify-packs` reads them from the installed package)*
- [x] CEM validated against the pinned schema and consumed by an independent CEM tool (e.g. an IDE custom-data generator or a third-party CEM reader) in a fixture outside the workspace *(Validated against `custom-elements-manifest@2.1.0` in `metadata.test.ts`. `scripts/verify-packs.mjs` installs the tarballs outside the workspace, follows each package's `customElements` field, and runs the third-party VS Code custom-data generator `custom-element-vs-code-integration@1.5.0` (pinned dev dependency) on it: every element, attribute, CSS property, and part of `@oddkin/components` (4 elements) and `@oddkin/ai` (24) reaches the HTML and CSS custom data, and `odd-button`'s `appearance` values come through as `primary`, `secondary`, `ghost`.)*
- [x] Contract tests: metadata defaults, allowed values, reflection, events, slots, parts match browser behavior → results attached as `verified` provenance *(`packages/components/test/contract.browser.test.ts` reads the published `custom-elements.json` and checks every `@oddkin/components` element in three engines: literal defaults, attribute → property conversion for every attribute and every allowed union value, removal back to the default, reflection exactly where `reflects` is declared, declared events equal the element's defined events, and every documented slot and part rendered by at least one authored example. Deliberate drift (a changed default, a removed `reflects`, an extra part) fails it. Results are attached as `verified` provenance: per-engine `conformance-result.v1` records tied to SHA-256 hashes of the tested manifest and implementation files. `npm run verify:metadata` attaches a fact only when it passed in all three engines against the current inputs, with browsers, versions, package, and input hashes as conditions; stale, failed, skipped, or malformed evidence is never attached (122 facts for the four elements). The same harness (`tests/contract/cem-contract.ts`) checks the 24 `@oddkin/ai` elements in three engines. Their slots and parts are rendered from the authored AI examples: the 76 slot and part facts recorded as not executed now pass, so 446 AI facts are verified (189 for the four components). The user-intent check runs on the 20 AI elements with user events: 34 `not-programmatic` facts pass, 481 AI facts are verified.)*
- [x] All module and registration references resolve against `exports` and built files *(`metadata.test.ts`: every CEM module path exists in `dist`, every registration is a package export; `verify-packs` now imports every JavaScript subpath of every package from its tarball)*
- [x] Minimal consumers exclude `components/models`, `core/model/schema`, the `components/composition` renderer, `core/inspect`, conformance/testing helpers, recipe presets, analyzer, Workbench, agent (the definition composer `compose()` is ordinary API) *(`packages/components/test/consumers.test.ts` follows imports across packages through their `exports` from every component, foundation, registration, and `./styles` entry and finds none of: model validators, `@oddkin/core/model`, the schema emitter, a composition renderer, recipe inspection, the recipe engine, layout presets, the token generator, Workbench, analyzer, or agent. It found `./side-nav` loading the recipe engine through `sideNavRecipe`; the recipe moved to the opt-in `@oddkin/components/side-nav/recipe`.)*
- [x] Foundation and docs enrichment for the slice: spacing (strategy inputs, steps, semantic vocabulary, declared precedence), colour roles, and radii referenced from `@oddkin/tokens` inspection output (no recomputation); component categories, `@useFor`/`@notFor`/`@limitation`, property → CSS property → token mappings where statically determinable; updated dates only from authored metadata or source history *(2026-10-19: the token build publishes the default theme's inspection as `@oddkin/tokens/inspect/default.json`, and `analyze({ tokens })` emits `foundations` from it: `foundation:spacing` (scale `space.*`, semantic vocabulary and component mappings from tokens aliasing a step), `foundation:color` (semantic roles, mode variation only for values keyed by the theme's modes), and `foundation:borders-and-radii` (widths, radii, mappings such as `card.radius` → `radius.lg`), each with its sections' availability and the tokens' origins (`preset`, `overridden`, …) with the inspection's package and export. Nothing is recomputed: no strategy is claimed, because the default theme configures none and its scale comes from presets. `packages/analyzer/test/foundations.test.ts` (known answers, and the published metadata equals what the published inspection yields); `verify-packs` reads the inspection from the installed tarball. Categories and `@useFor`/`@notFor`/`@limitation` were already published. 2026-10-19 (styles): `components[…].styleMappings` traces options and states to tokens, e.g. `control | appearance="primary" (enabled, hover) @media (hover: hover) | background → color.action.hover`. Every style source's CSS is read in cascade order — `css` templates, `unsafeCSS` literals, and the `css` text each package now publishes in its `styles` facts — so `@oddkin/ai` reads through the sheets `@oddkin/components` shares. A mapping is the declaration that wins by `!important`, layer, specificity, and order for its part, condition, and property; a property that any rule outside the grammar may set stays unmapped. All 4 components (120 mappings) and 24 AI elements (248) carry them. `packages/analyzer/test/style-mappings.test.ts` (grammar, cascade, layers, unread rules) and both packages' metadata tests; 21 of 21 mutants killed. 2026-10-20 (dates): `docs[*].updated` for the `components/<tag>` and `foundations/<id>` pages: an authored `@updated` date, or the latest commit touching the page's declared sources (registration, class chain, explicit metadata, definitions, template, styles, examples; for foundations the theme sources the build declares), never build time and none in a shallow clone. Workbench's page registry shows each date on the same page. `packages/analyzer/test/docs.test.ts` (11 tests, with temporary git repositories and a shallow clone) and the components metadata test; 19 mutants, 18 killed, and the survivor exposed two redundant package guards, which were removed.)*

**Acceptance:** browser, form, accessible-name, keyboard, theme, and packed-consumer checks pass; state stays externally owned; side-nav (recipe + data), editable list, and card pass in Chromium, Firefox, WebKit; CEM validates and works with an independent consumer; metadata matches implementation and exports.

### M5 — Workbench vertical slice and docs slice

Design: [workbench.md](./docs/architecture/workbench.md), [workbench-pages.md](./docs/architecture/workbench-pages.md). Slice scope: `odd-button`, `odd-input`, `odd-card`, `odd-side-nav`; Foundations overview and the Spacing detail page; Components catalog; Theme Studio destination.

**Workbench shell**

- [x] Extend the M3 helper-only `@oddkin/workbench` package with its application shell and public panels; zero third-party runtime deps in the browser bundle. Integrate state/app only after M6–M7. *(Shell: `@oddkin/workbench/app` (`OddkinWorkbench`) and the registering `./app/define` entry, `<oddkin-workbench>` in the light DOM on `@oddkin/app` (`createApp`, `createRouter` with routes generated from the page registry, `pageLifecycle`); its browser graph is Oddkin packages only (`npm run lint:boundaries`). Panels (2026-10-21): `./panels/component`, `./panels/foundation`, and `./panels/catalog`, each with a `/define` entry (table in workbench.md §2.1): open shadow roots, inputs as properties, plain links, no app root or router; `panels.browser.test.ts` (7 tests × 3 engines); every export imports in Node from the built package; the boundary checker passes over the built artifacts.)*
- [~] `oddkin.config.ts` / `defineWorkbenchConfig`; analyzer outputs + config → indexes and page registry; module map → per-frame import maps; assets/fonts/themes from config; custom prefix and branding *(Part 1: DOM-free `@oddkin/workbench/data` ([workbench.md](./docs/architecture/workbench.md) §2.1). `buildWorkbenchData` turns one or more packages' published CEM and enrichment, authored content, and a token inspection into a small index (registry, branding, libraries, diagnostics) and one data file per visible page. A component page carries its CEM declaration, enrichment, and examples verbatim; a foundation page carries its metadata and the listed tokens from the inspection, unknown values marked `missing` and never guessed. Records shared by several packages merge by id, and conflicts keep the first and are diagnosed. Output is byte-stable, with no build time. `createImportMap` builds import maps from package `exports` with Node's condition order, `development` first; exact patterns become prefixes, and what an import map cannot express is diagnosed. Part 2: `@oddkin/workbench/node` loads `oddkin.config.*` (TypeScript through Node's type stripping), resolves the documented packages and their dependencies as Node does, reads their manifests, enrichment, the token inspection their foundations reference (through that package's `exports`), and `oddkin.docs.*`, and builds the data files, the frames' and the shell's import maps under versioned package URLs (Node-only dependencies kept out of the shell), and the shell page. Evidence: `packages/workbench/test/data.test.ts` (22 tests) and `node-inputs.test.ts` (17 tests, including an import map that resolves every bare specifier of the real components' module graph); 34 of 35 (part 1, 1 equivalent) and 32 of 32 (part 2) mutants killed. Since 2026-10-21 the frame document links the configured `assets` given as package export specifiers (stylesheets linked, fonts preloaded), or by default `@oddkin/tokens/css/default.css` when the documented libraries depend on it, and diagnoses unusable ones; the Node tooling loads each package's `defineExamples` modules named in its metadata as preset data (`node-inputs.test.ts`). Remaining: project-relative asset files and `branding.theme`. The shell shows the configured branding (name and logo) since 2026-10-21's verification.)*
- [~] `oddkin analyze` and `oddkin workbench dev` (analyze + watch + validate + live updates); `.oddkin/` generated dir with marker; authored files never touched *(The `oddkin` bin in `@oddkin/workbench` ([workbench.md](./docs/architecture/workbench.md) §2.1, §10): `oddkin analyze` runs `@oddkin/analyzer`'s own command with the same arguments and exit code; `oddkin workbench dev` validates the configuration and inputs, serves the shell page, the data, and package files from one origin (history-mode fallback; hash mode only at the entry, like a static host; traversal and links out of a package refused), writes `.oddkin/workbench/` with a marker and refuses an unmarked directory, watches the configuration, content, manifests, metadata, and package files, and after each rebuild sends an `update` event over Server-Sent Events naming the changed index, pages, modules, or shell page; an invalid edit keeps the last valid data and reports diagnostics. Evidence: `node-generated.test.ts` (5 tests), `node-server.test.ts` (6, over HTTP and a real event stream), `node-cli.test.ts` (5, including the built command in a real Node process); 25 of 25 mutants killed. ### M6 — Stores, context, and app ownership

- [x] `createStore`/`update`/`select`, immutable snapshots, batching, reset/dispose, optional action instrumentation, stable external subscriptions *(`@oddkin/state/store`: a core signal holds the snapshot behind a read-only computed `state`; memoized `select` with an equality option and `shallowEqual`; `update`, `reset`, `dispose`; `action()` batches and records names; snapshots frozen in both builds, `freeze: false` to opt out; `getSnapshot`/`subscribe` are the shared external-store contract. `packages/state/test/store.test.ts` (28 Node tests): selected-value notification, laziness and observation-only registration, glitch-free combination with other signals, actions batch but do not roll back, throwing updaters and selectors, own-store reentrancy rejected, subscriber reentrancy and the runaway guard, freezing (only new parts, cycles, 100,000-deep nesting, no getters, maps, sets, and class instances untouched), reset, dispose; `production.test.ts` (6): equivalent builds, stable codes without messages. 15 of 15 mutants killed. The store graph is 2,214 B gzip with the reactive graph (budget 2,560 B) and loads no DOM module.)*
- [x] Readables integrate via the existing graph and `Scope` *(`state` and selectors are core readables, so the effects and bindings that read them are owned by their `Scope`; `scope.add(store.subscribe(…))` for raw subscriptions. `store.test.ts` (scope ownership back to baseline, no runs after disposal); `store.browser.test.ts` in three engines: a selector bound directly updates one text node without re-rendering, a render-tracked selector re-renders only for its own value, nothing runs while disconnected, `scopeStats()` returns to baseline, and React's `useSyncExternalStore` reads the store and releases it on unmount. Context consumers are readables too (§7.2 of core-contracts.md).)*
- [x] Typed event-based context: nearest provider, overrides, independent roots, Shadow DOM, reparenting, provider replacement *(`@oddkin/state/context` on the community context protocol (`context-request`, plus `context-provider` announcements as in Lit): `createContext`, `provide` (a value or a readable to follow), `consume` (a tracked readable with `fallback`, `provided`, `required`, `resolve()`), controllers for `scope.use()`; Oddkin peers share the provider's readable, other implementations get callbacks. `packages/state/test/context.browser.test.ts` (13 tests; Chromium, Firefox, WebKit, with the `moveBefore()` case in Chromium and Firefox only): nearest provider and nested overrides, direct and render-tracked reads, fallback, `state.context.missing`, a provider defined after its consumers, independent roots, consumers inside shadow roots and slotted children of a shadow-root provider, reparenting, state-preserving moves with `resolve()`, a provider stopping (consumers go to the next provider up, or wait and take a new one), an element consuming and providing the same context, protocol consumers and providers in both directions, ownership back to baseline. 13 of 13 mutants killed. Provider and consumer: 3,699 B gzip with the reactive graph and `Scope` (budget 4,096 B).)*
- [x] Persistence and inspection as separate entries *(Inspection: `@oddkin/state/inspect` — `inspect()` keeps a bounded log of updates with their action causes and live subscriber counts, and stops when disposed or when the store is; `storeStats()` counts live stores and subscriptions. Stores carry only counters and one observer hook. Persistence: `@oddkin/state/persist` — `persist(store, { key, version, select, restore, migrate, validate, … })` restores before subscribing, writes the selection once per microtask when it changed, and isolates storage failures; `packages/state/test/persist.test.ts` (22 Node tests) and `persist.browser.test.ts` (real Web Storage in three engines, including a real `QuotaExceededError`); 14 of 14 mutants killed; a persisted store is 3,028 B gzip (budget 3,328 B). Neither is re-exported from the root entry.)*
- [x] Document external-store subscription semantics, nested snapshot updates, provider absence/reparenting, bounded inspection, persistence selection/versioning, and reset/migration behavior. *([core-contracts.md](./docs/architecture/core-contracts.md) §7.1: subscription semantics and reentrancy, nested snapshot updates, reset, bounded inspection; §7.2: provider absence, reparenting, and replacement; §7.3: persistence selection, versions and migrations, restore ordering, `clear()` as the reset, and every failure code.)*
- [x] `createApp()` start/stop/root ownership; restart; two isolated apps on one page *(`@oddkin/app/app`, re-exported from the root ([core-contracts.md](./docs/architecture/core-contracts.md) §9): a fresh root `Scope` per run, services returned by `setup`, `provide()` at the root and `use()` for services; idempotent `start()`/`stop()`; a throwing `setup` leaves the application stopped with partial work disposed; reentrant calls rejected (`app.lifecycle.reentrant`); one running application per root (`app.root.claimed`). `packages/app/test/app.test.ts` (10 Node tests) and `app.browser.test.ts` (4 tests × 3 engines): context at the root follows stop and restart (consumers connected before the start resolve, fall back, and resolve to the next run), two applications keep contexts, timers, and stopping apart, `scopeStats()` returns to baseline, a nested application overrides below its root, a root is released after stop and after a failed start; `production.test.ts` (6). 13 of 13 mutants killed. An application root with a store provided as context is 4,767 B gzip (budget 5,120 B); component graphs still load no state or app code (`npm run size`).)*

**Acceptance:** selectors notify only on selected-value change; disposal stops work; context follows providers; storage failures isolated; component-only consumers import no state/app code.

### M7 — Resources, navigation, real async UI, static builds

- [x] Resource: inert construction, activation, invalidation, fresh request scopes, discriminated state, refresh/cancel/retry, freshness policy *(`@oddkin/app/resource`, re-exported from `@oddkin/app` ([core-contracts.md](./docs/architecture/core-contracts.md) §8): inert until `scope.use()`; the source is followed by an effect owned by that scope; each generation gets a fresh request scope and signal; `idle`/`pending`/`success`/`error` with the latest success as `previous`; `refresh`, `retry`, `cancel` (back to the prior state, no error), `dispose`; `freshFor` reuses a fresh success on reconnection, otherwise reconnecting reloads. Not implemented: automatic retries, debounce, caching, deduplication, stale-while-revalidate, optimistic state, and the restore policy (with page suspension). 3,416 B gzip for a search resource (budget 3,840 B).)*
- [x] Race, abort-ignoring, disposal-during-parse, abort-vs-error, timeout, reconnect tests *(`packages/app/test/resource.test.ts` (15 Node tests): a new key aborts the previous generation and a late result never wins, including a superseded load that resolves before the current one or fails after it succeeded; a result whose owner disconnected during parsing is discarded; an aborted load's rejection is neither an error nor reported, while a real failure is an `error` that keeps `previous`; a timeout fails with a `TimeoutError` distinct from `cancel()`'s `AbortError`; the owner's disconnect reason reaches the load; request scopes end with their generation; reconnection with and without `freshFor`; equal keys; untracked loads, including `refresh()` from an effect. `resource.browser.test.ts` (3 engines): the search element of §8 needs no cleanup code, keeps stale responses out, cancels on disconnect with the element's reason, reloads on reconnect, returns `scopeStats()` to baseline, and leaves no unhandled rejection. 14 of 14 mutants killed.)*
- [x] Router: parsing, history, lazy/nested routes, errors, URL as source of truth, per-navigation ownership *(`@oddkin/app/router`, re-exported from `@oddkin/app` ([core-contracts.md](./docs/architecture/core-contracts.md) §9): `route()` with static, `:param`, rest, and index patterns per level, typed `params` (declining a match) and `search`; DOM-free `matchRoutes()`; `createRouter()` as an application service that reads the URL and follows `popstate`; new levels load in parallel with their own scopes and commit together, writing the history entry only then, while unchanged outer levels keep their scope, data, and match; superseded navigations are aborted, never commit, and leave no entry; failing levels, throwing parsers, failed `import()`s, not-found, and redirects (pushed or replaced like the navigation; loops end in `app.router.redirect`); `refresh()`. Tests: `router.test.ts` (10 Node tests) and `router.browser.test.ts` (9 tests × 3 engines); 38 of 38 mutants killed. 5,461 B gzip for a router (budget 6,144 B).)*
- [x] Focus/title/announcement/scroll; native link escapes; base path; hash mode *(Titles from the deepest level through `formatTitle`; document-level link interception with native escapes (modifiers, non-primary buttons, `target`, `download`, `rel="external"`, other origins, outside the base path, page fragments, already-handled clicks; links in open shadow roots); an explicit `base` with `href()`; `mode: 'hash'` with in-page fragments that keep the route. After a navigation to another path: focus moves to the application's `focus(state)` target and the title (or `announce(state)`) is announced through core's `LiveAnnouncer`; query and fragment changes keep focus. Scroll restoration: top for a new path, fragments scrolled into view, positions kept per history entry (in memory, and in the entry once scrolling pauses, since Chromium and Firefox drop `pagehide` writes on reload) and restored on back, forward, and reload; a refused history write (WebKit's 100-per-10-seconds limit) abandons the navigation. Verified in `router-after.browser.test.ts` (5 tests × 3 engines), `router.test.ts`, and `e2e/router.spec.ts`; 23 of 23 mutants killed. 6,974 B gzip (budget 7,680 B).)*
- [x] Docs app and Workbench shell on Oddkin's router; directory/detail recipe with all async states *(2026-10-21: both shells use `@oddkin/app` routing; docs directory/detail routes expose pending, error, retry, and not-found states. `e2e/docs.spec.ts`: 12 tests across Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit; see [verification.md](./docs/verification.md).)*
- [x] `oddkin workbench build`: metadata validation, static app with assets, lazy imports, deep links, configurable base path, page-registry legacy redirects (redirect stubs in history mode, in-app redirects in hash mode); never publishes/deploys; deterministic output *(2026-10-27, `packages/workbench/src/node/static.ts` (workbench.md §10): writes the dev server's layout into a marked directory, `--base`/`--routing` override `hosting`; configuration or metadata errors write nothing, and an import-map target a package does not publish is reported; the panels and Theme Studio stay lazy imports as in dev. Legacy routes: in history mode each legacy path is a copy of the shell whose router redirects, instead of a meta-refresh stub, because `/playground?component=<tag>` needs its query. Deterministic: a rebuild writes nothing and removes stale files. `test/node-build.test.ts`, 5 tests.)*
- [~] Static build served under a subpath: deep links, refresh, redirects, share links, Theme Studio editing/generation/export work with no Node or analyzer backend *(2026-10-27, `e2e/workbench-static.spec.ts`: the build of `tests/fixtures/workbench-oddkin` under `/tests/fixtures/workbench-oddkin/.oddkin/static-<engine>/` from `scripts/serve-static.mjs` (no rewrites), 4 tests in Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit: deep link and refresh with the preview frame, navigation and back/forward inside the base path, the base URL to the first destination, `/playground?component=odd-input&preset=x` to `components/odd-input?preset=x`, `404.html` as Page not found, a copied example link restoring its configuration, and Theme Studio generating in its worker and applying an override to the real button. Remaining: export, which Theme Studio does not have yet (M9). Found on the way: Theme Studio's `specimen` requests import module graphs like `mount` but had the 10 s request timeout; they now share the mount timeout (`frame.browser.test.ts`).)*
- [~] Verify direct-path fallback versus hash routing honestly for the chosen host; test lazy-load failures, native anchor escapes, back/forward, stale navigation, focus/scroll, and available static guide content. *(`e2e/router-hosting.spec.ts` runs `examples/router` on four emulated host classes in Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit. Rewrites: deep paths, reloads, and back/forward across documents work, with 200 also for unknown paths. GitHub Pages' `404.html`: deep links work but answer 404. One document per known route: 200 after a `/x` → `/x/` redirect. No fallback: a deep link, and Back to an in-app entry once its document is gone, get the server's 404, while hash mode works. Lazy routes: a module failing with a network error, 404, or 503 is the route's error, and `refresh()` loads it in place in all three engines; a module a later deploy removed fails on every host, including a rewrite's HTML; a slow lazy load superseded by a link never commits and leaves no entry. Also covered: native escapes from deep documents, a deep link to a fragment, and no focus or announcement on the first load. The history-mode document now uses base-rooted URLs, which a fallback requires. 5 of 5 e2e mutants killed. Remaining: the host itself, still "to be chosen" in §9 (verified here against emulations of documented behaviour, not deployed), and static guide content with the docs app (869).)*
- [x] Command/mutation examples distinguish cancellation from rollback and use an explicit retry policy. *(The cart element of [core-contracts.md](./docs/architecture/core-contracts.md) §8.1, run in `packages/app/test/command.browser.test.ts` in three engines: an optimistic item is rolled back only when the server refuses it (a 409, not retried); a 503 is retried under the policy with the same idempotent item; cancelling rolls nothing back, reads the cart again, and shows the item the server added anyway; disconnecting cancels the pending addition with the element's reason and returns `scopeStats()` to baseline, with no unhandled rejection. `command.test.ts` (18 Node tests) covers ownership, outcomes, `exclusive`/`queue`/`latest`, retry limits, delays, throwing or invalid policies, timeouts, `reset`, `dispose`, and reentrancy; 28 of 28 mutants killed. 3,723 B gzip for a command with a retry policy (budget 4,096 B).)*
- [x] Page suspension and restoration (core-contracts.md §9.1, ADR 0035): explicit `@oddkin/app` lifecycle service; service suspend/resume; resource restore policies; browser fixtures proving actual persisted restoration (`pageshow.persisted`) distinct from reload and ordinary history navigation; repeated restoration, state preservation, listener counts, resource freshness, and explicit shutdown tested separately; environments that prevent real cache restoration recorded as not verified; no `unload` listeners; components inside other frameworks unaffected *(`e2e/lifecycle.spec.ts` on `examples/lifecycle`: in Chromium, which the test launches as the full build without Playwright's cache-disabling switch, two real restorations in a row (`pageshow.persisted` true, the same document) keep the count, suspend the polling service at `pagehide` before Chromium's `freeze` and resume it once at `pageshow` after `resume`, register no listener, scope, or timer twice, and reload only the resource with `revalidateOnRestore`; after an explicit stop a restoration resumes and reloads nothing. In all three engines: the service registers its listeners once and nothing registers `unload`; a reload is a new document; history navigation inside the page suspends nothing; stop removes the listeners and start adds them once. Not verified: a real restoration in Firefox (Playwright's driver disables the cache; overriding its preference lost the restored page) and WebKit (never restored, headless or headed), and freezing outside the cache (headless Chromium ignored CDP's `Page.setWebLifecycleState`). Components: importing every core, components, state, and app entry and connecting `odd-input`, `odd-button`, `odd-card`, and `odd-side-nav` registers no lifecycle listener (three engines). 4,676 B gzip for an application with the service (budget 5,120 B).)*

**Acceptance:** search example needs no hand-written cleanup; stale data never wins; page suspension never disposes the app and restoration duplicates no listeners; navigation under base paths works; static Workbench build under a subpath retains full Theme Studio editing.

**Theme Studio completion** ([workbench.md §6–§9](./docs/architecture/workbench.md))

- [ ] Controls for every category, strategy-aware; labels for foundation/generated/alias/override; density vs typography vs target size kept distinct
- [ ] Specimens: replaceable recipe; light/dark panels; surface, heading, body, link, badge, labelled input, checkbox, switch, primary/secondary actions, navigation, composed layouts; rest/hover/pressed/focus-visible/disabled/invalid/custom states via interaction or supported fixtures, otherwise labelled token specimens
- [ ] Role mapping: automatic only when unambiguous (declared `@previewRole` or adapter); picker with compatibility diagnostics; adapters over public props/slots/events/fixtures/Scope; saved compositions (versioned)
- [ ] Token inspection: type, configured/generated/override values, alias chain, provenance, variations, computed value in selected preview, declared/potential/observed references (static reference ≠ proof of effect)
- [ ] Visualizations: ramps, light/dark roles, contrast pairs (unknown contexts labelled; not certification), spacing, typography/line height, radius/border/elevation, control sizing/density, alias graph, baseline-vs-draft diff; linked to config fields and consumers
- [ ] Layout roles, conditions, profiles editable with resizable container previews
- [ ] Comparison: baseline vs draft; multiple independent and nested scopes; product/density/direction/profile selection; viewport/container resizing
- [ ] Reset, undo/redo, presets; versioned validated share URLs (non-serializable values excluded with a note); config import/export; CSS/SCSS/JSON/TS exports via the canonical generator only
- [ ] Explicit dev-mode "Save to file" with diff and confirmation; browser never silently overwrites source
- [ ] Standard-CEM baseline: CSS-custom-property editing without generator, clearly labelled
- [ ] Condition/profile edits regenerate only relevant preview-owned generated CSS; do not mutate reusable component static sheets. Verify Worker coalescing, stale-work cost, bounded history, and truthful export pending/error state.
- [ ] Complete keyboard/screen-reader operation of editors, comparison panels, swatches/graphs, mappings, validation messages, and exports; state/value information is available without color alone.
- [ ] Theme Studio page structure from capabilities (Generate · Ramps and roles · Live preview · Contrast · Color vision · Comparison · Inspection · Exports · Method and limits) with an optional sticky live-result summary; remains a primary destination

**Foundation detail pages (all categories)** ([workbench-pages.md §5](./docs/architecture/workbench-pages.md))

- [ ] Token architecture, Color, Typography, Borders and radii, Elevation, Motion, Themes, Responsive behavior pages generated from the canonical token outputs and enrichment, each section only when supplied; tables, specimens, alias chains, variations, interactive motion demos with reduced-motion behavior, resizable responsive examples, usage snippets; Themes links to Theme Studio
- [ ] Every value-bearing section labels its context (baseline by default; explicit labelled draft); computed pixels only with an explicit preview context
- [ ] Built-in explanatory copy for each concept, phrased without asserting library-specific facts; authored overrides per page/section

**Products and modes (optional)** ([workbench-pages.md §9](./docs/architecture/workbench-pages.md))

- [ ] Disabled by default: no route, navigation, card, search entry, or comparison content
- [ ] When enabled: configurable placement; product definitions validated against the product schema; sources from local theme config, installed packages, or versioned manifests fetched and snapshotted at build time; diagnostics for unavailable/incompatible sources
- [ ] Product comparison composed with mode/density/profile using the canonical generator and isolated previews; product identity kept distinct from mode, theme, density, profiles
- [ ] Workspace discovery (`oddkin workbench products --discover`) reports suggestions with reasons; excludes tooling, docs apps, tests, examples, internal packages by default; never enables or publishes a page
- [ ] Product-aware impact reporting: token, preset, and product-configuration changes report affected products, families, and examples with reasons; verification selection across 1/2/3/10-product configurations
- [ ] Styling explanations in Workbench and Theme Studio (`explanation.v1` §4.1): observed computed value, source-unit token value, component variable, semantic role, alias chain, scope context, overrides, recipe/condition relationships, provenance and limitations; condition activity only when reliably observed; measurements only through explicit inspection; correct in nested product scopes
- [ ] DTCG Resolver experiment and decision (§5.1)
- [ ] Any number of products: searchable product selection, comparison of user-chosen products with only those previews mounted, per-product assets loaded on demand, deselected previews disposed; verified with 1-, 2-, 3-, and 10-product fixtures, including selection, comparison, loading, cleanup, and preview resource usage
- [ ] Additive `product.v1` fields for design language, component family, and foundation compatibility ranges; `product.foundation.incompatible` diagnostics from installed or supplied artifacts without checking out other repositories
- [ ] Token generator: product-specific semantic role overrides and several themes (design languages) per document, with nested-scope verification across shadow roots, slotted content, overlays/popovers, relocated components, and adopted documents; CSS growth re-measured for 1/2/3/10 products; no product × mode × density × profile emission
- [ ] Shared and different design languages; shared and product-specific component families; independent and nested product/theme scopes; product-specific focus visibility, contrast, target size, direction, reduced motion, and forced colours where supported

**Acceptance:** exported config and styles reproduce the preview in an independent consumer; SCSS consumer follows scoped themes; base-colour, spacing, sizing, and typography changes affect compatible components correctly; overrides and unrelated roles preserved; library components replace specimen roles with useful diagnostics; CSS-only adoption still needs no Oddkin JS; every supported foundation page renders from data with honest absence elsewhere; Products and modes is absent unless enabled and its source paths work in static builds.

In no other module graph (`autoload.test.ts`). `examples/autoload/` (autoload and explicit pages, strict CSP and Trusted Types) and the packed consumer `autoload`; `e2e/autoload.spec.ts` 9 passed in three engines: the same elements defined, two more module requests (72 against 70; packed 71 against 69), and time to defined noisy under parallel workers (packed: Chromium 507 against 409 ms, Firefox 1,369 against 1,932, WebKit 413 against 329). When to use which: `packages/components/README.md`.)*
- [~] **CSS contracts for new components** (css-architecture.md §8–§12): shared positioning controller with native anchor positioning per detected subfeature, one backend at a time, observers owned by the overlay's `Scope` (F09, F10); `popover` and `<dialog>` top layer with explicit close controls (iOS floor exception) and inert backgrounds; enter/exit transitions with `@starting-style` behind gates and reduced motion, never animation-dependent cleanup (F11); textarea `field-sizing` over a native baseline (F12); `odd-select` on a styled native `<select>` with `appearance: base-select` as a gated enhancement and the WebKit strict-CSP `<select>` report accounted for (F13); `:open` behind `selector()` gates; `accent-color`, `scrollbar-gutter`, `overscroll-behavior` as literal enhancements; constrained-content, media-frame, scroll-region, and subgrid primitives from demonstrated needs; `text-wrap: balance`/`pretty` for headings and text; component-private `@property` registrations only under the written policy. Each adoption updates features.json with tests of both paths. *(2026-10-23, Agent 2: the popover top layer with an explicit close control and placement by the shared controller while open (observers in a per-open scope); popovers fade in with a keyframes animation at the floor and out behind the `transition-behavior: allow-discrete` gate, nothing under reduced motion, and no cleanup waits for a transition; registry entries `popover-api` and `starting-style` implemented with tests. Then the `<dialog>` top layer with an inert background (`odd-dialog`, shown modally; it and its backdrop fade like popovers), `closedby` as an enhancement over an emulated baseline, and dvh maximum sizes; registry entries `dialog-element`, `dialog-closedby`, and `dynamic-viewport-units` implemented with tests. 2026-10-23, Agent 3: `odd-select` with `appearance: base-select` as a gated enhancement is verified on both paths (F13, css-architecture.md §16.1). Remaining: `:open` (dialogs keep their `[open]` attribute, which says the same at the floor), the native anchor backend, and the rest of this list.)*
- [~] Controllers extracted only from demonstrated shared needs *(2026-10-22: `@oddkin/core/controllers/typeahead`, `active-descendant`, and `resize`, each for patterns several M10 elements share: typeahead for listbox, menu, and tree; active descendant for listbox, tree, combobox, and command palette; resize for the split panel (and the deferred data grid's column resizing). Remaining: the §2.2 collection source, then the consumers (component-contracts.md §3.3, §3.4, §3.6, §3.7).)*
- [ ] Conformance suites per catalog component with assertions, invariants, mutants, and accepted customizations; published under `@oddkin/workbench/conformance/*` with versions tied to their foundation contracts
- [~] Declare each component's supported customization levels; recomposable components ship layered exports, a foundation entry, a typed anatomy contract, documented structural styles, and shared interaction fixtures; behavior controllers with explicit host requirements only where templates differ substantially and evidence justifies them *(Agent 4, 2026-10-26: `@customization 1 2 3` on the 14 display elements, odd-combobox, and odd-picker, as odd-side-nav declared it: each ships a foundation entry and structural styles, and its level-3 derived implementation (`test/derived.ts`) runs through the contract tests; metadata `customization.levels` is authored for them. No element declares 4 (no typed anatomy contract) or 5. Agent 1, 2026-10-27: the other 36 elements declare 1 2 3, except odd-tab-panel (2 3, no look of its own); `customization.test.ts` checks what each declared level ships, which found three foundations short a structural sheet (picker, checkbox, input), now exported. Remaining: typed anatomy contracts (level 4) and shared interaction fixtures.)*
- [ ] Extend analyzer and example coverage to each catalog component as it lands: authored design guidance (when appropriate, constraints, labelling and accessibility responsibilities, content relationships and companions, token roles and customization paths, recipes and responsive expectations) with validated references, and correct/incorrect example pairs for its common mistakes *(Deferred (owner, 2026-10-27), still open: guidance and correct/incorrect example pairs for every element; listed in §11.)*

**Per-component definition of done:** typed runtime definition (statically analyzable) · analyzer-generated CEM + enrichment entry with provenance · defaults and allowed values · property/attribute/event-detail contracts · declared sync pairs · slots, parts, CSS variables, custom states, roles and tokens used · preview roles · recipe and container behavior · data model (if structured) · accessibility/keyboard/form guidance · known limitations · category, use-for/not-for, and property → CSS property → token mappings where determinable · `*.examples.ts` (default + key variants + presets + fixtures where needed) · Workbench detail page (documentation + interactive example + editable API table) renders with no gaps beyond labelled ones · docs page, source link · browser and lifetime tests driven by shared examples · narrow/wide check · metadata-vs-implementation contract check · agent-kit reference generated with validated guidance references (missing guidance labelled unavailable) · supported customization levels declared; recomposable components verified with a derived definition against the same interaction fixtures · conformance suite with mutants and evidence records.

**Acceptance:** a release candidate installs and works without workspace access, private imports, unexpected deps, or undocumented transforms; docs and Workbench demonstrate released APIs and state tested limits.

## 7. Global acceptance criteria

| # | Criterion | Evidence in |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | One reactive engine powers local state, readables, selectors, resources; state/app optional for components | M3, M6, M8 |
| 2 | Listeners/timers/observers need no mirrored manual cleanup; teardown, reconnect, moves, adoption follow contracts | M3, M8 |
| 3 | Effect reruns and resource supersession release old work; stale results cannot commit | M3, M7 |
| 4 | Batching and derived reads are predictable; direct bindings avoid unnecessary renders without losing DOM identity | M3, M8 |
| 5 | Templates preserve focus, selection, native control state, keyed identity, cleanup; diagnostics identify binding and phase | M3, M8 |
| 6 | Properties/events/attributes work in plain HTML and tested frameworks; no Oddkin store mandatory | M4, M12 |
| 7 | Forms, accessible names, keyboard, browser differences have evidence beyond automated scans | M4, M8 |
| 8 | Small config → deterministic tokens; exact spacing outputs with preserved units and stable names; actionable diagnostics | M2 |
| 9 | CSS-only consumer needs no Oddkin JS; SCSS follows scoped themes; CSS-file and CSS-in-TS equivalent; literals explicit | M2, M4, M9 |
| 10 | Typed reference imports are DOM-free and registration-free | M1, M4 |
| 11 | One sheet per style definition per document; nested aliases and scopes correct across shadow roots | M3, M8 |
| 12 | Optional and tooling imports absent from minimal fixtures; boundary and runtime-dep rules hold in emitted artifacts | M1, M8, M12 |
| 13 | Exported themes and copied snippets work in independent consumers | M5, M9, M11, M12 |
| 14 | Docs, controls, generated code, and implementation stay aligned | M5, M10, M11 |
| 15 | Framework examples exercise the actual state, routing, resource APIs | M7, M11 |
| 16 | Benchmark and usability results back any published claim, including unfavorable results | M8, M12 |
| 17 | Keyboard, form, contrast, resizing, reduced-motion, and browser checks recorded accurately | M8, M12 |
| 18 | Layout recipes produce reusable CSS with documented defaults, precedence, diagnostics; overrides never mutate shared styles | M3, M4, M8 |
| 19 | Container-responsive layout works without JS resize handling across slots and nested custom elements | M4, M8 |
| 20 | Roles, conditions, profiles validate, emit deterministically, re-resolve in nested scopes, never shrink text/targets below minimums | M2, M8, M9 |
| 21 | Same role contracts usable from native elements, custom components, CSS, SCSS, TS | M4, M9, M11 |
| 22 | Typed local objects and validated external JSON; path-precise diagnostics; version migrations | M3, M4 |
| 23 | Data-driven, declarative, hybrid authoring equivalent; region sources documented and diagnosed | M4, M10 |
| 24 | Collections preserve identity/focus/state; external reactive changes update unchanged items; scopes released; repeated mount/unmount returns to baseline | M3, M4, M8 |
| 25 | Controlled vs uncontrolled state and precedence never create competing sources of truth; caller data never mutated | M4, M8 |
| 25a | Data-driven components work from plain JS and tested framework consumers without Oddkin state, with responsive recipes and nested themes; minimal consumers exclude unused model validators, schema emitters, and composition modules | M4, M8, M12 |
| 26 | **Standard CEM validates against the pinned schema and works with an independent consumer; enrichment is separate and versioned** | M4, M12 |
| 27 | **Metadata is extracted without executing modules, matches implementation and published exports, and labels every fact's provenance; dynamic definitions are diagnosed** | M3, M4, M8 |
| 28 | **Basic Workbench exploration works without authored stories; standard-CEM libraries get the documented baseline; missing enrichment is labelled, never invented** | M5, M11 |
| 29 | **Controls handle property vs attribute, nullable, falsy, read-only, unset, and reset correctly; complex values use schemas, fixtures, or adapters** | M5, M8 |
| 30 | **Preview registries, styles, failures, and lifecycle resources are isolated; switching components/examples disposes previous scopes** | M5, M8 |
| 31 | **Theme Studio uses the canonical config, validation, and generator; CLI and browser outputs are identical; edits update real components without remount, preserve state, keep overrides/unrelated roles, keep the last valid preview on invalid drafts, and latest generation wins** | M2, M5, M9 |
| 32 | **Library components map to specimen roles via declared capabilities or adapters with useful diagnostics; fallbacks labelled** | M5, M9 |
| 33 | **Token provenance, computed values, references (declared/potential/observed), state specimens, and verification labels are accurate; contrast results are not presented as certification** | M9 |
| 34 | **Shared `defineExamples` drive Workbench, docs, browser tests, Theme Studio, and agents** | M4, M5, M10, M11 |
| 35 | **Analyzer watch mode and Workbench builds are deterministic and preserve authored files; static builds work under a subpath with full Theme Studio editing and no backend** | M5, M7, M11 |
| 36 | `@oddkin/agent` is dev-only; operations return exact installed contracts with provenance; agent output passes the same checks as handwritten code | M11, M12 |
| 37 | Milestone build order is valid; examples/helpers do not create production cycles, and the first Workbench runs before optional state/router services exist | M1, M3–M7 |
| 38 | Render-tracked `.value` reads, direct readable bindings, untracked snapshots, public-property bindings, dual-path coalescing, and update/readiness boundaries are understandable and verified | M3, M8 |
| 39 | Failure ownership and development/production diagnostics are explicit; no silent errors, permanent stopped bindings without recovery guidance, or mandatory debug machinery | M3, M8, M12 |
| 40 | Registry collisions/version limits are diagnosed; protocol, realm, and duplicate-package behavior match the published compatibility policy | M3, M5, M8, M12 |
| 41 | External stores/frameworks integrate through stable public properties/events/subscriptions and optional typed/form adapters without Oddkin application state | M4, M6, M12 |
| 42 | Container/slot/shadow/cascade behavior and live threshold edits match native CSS constraints and verified baseline syntax | M2, M4, M8, M9 |
| 43 | Native/custom form strategies and limits are explicit; submitter, validation, reset, keyboard, and naming behavior have browser evidence | M4, M8, M12 |
| 44 | Themes preserve units/user font preferences; first paint, overrides, nested aliases, output size, and supported DTCG mappings are independently verified | M2, M8, M9 |
| 45 | Workbench/docs are keyboard/screen-reader usable, localized through documented contracts, lazy-loaded, and bounded in frames/logs/history | M5, M9, M11, M12 |
| 46 | Architecture case studies, static-readable/versioned docs decisions, actionable diagnostics, and novice/agent adoption paths support the released product | M5, M11, M12 |
| 47 | Every research task has a direct primary-source starting link and a recorded decision/evidence status; existing completion claims are not upgraded without verification | M0–M12 |
| 48 | **Foundations matches the reference's card/navigation hierarchy (eyebrow, title, summary, card grid, contextual sidebar) using each library's actual data; detail pages include applicable tables, specimens, aliases, explanations, anchors, and examples; unsupported sections are absent** | M5, M9 |
| 49 | **Different library configurations produce different, correct content without editing application templates; nothing from the reference (inventory, taxonomy, token names, values, claims) is hardcoded** | M5, M9, M11 |
| 50 | **Card dates, tags, counts, and verification statements are accurate or omitted; dates come only from declared metadata or source history, never build time** | M4, M5, M11 |
| 51 | **Configuration changes regenerate content without overwriting authored guidance; generated facts stay authoritative over prose** | M5, M11 |
| 52 | **Components keeps searchable/filterable catalog navigation with counts, configurable categories, fallback group, and URL state; cards load no component modules** | M5, M11 |
| 53 | **Each component detail page combines documentation, editable API table, slots, preview settings, presets, reset, sharing, and reproducible plain HTML/JS code; there is no standalone Playground destination** | M5, M10, M11 |
| 54 | **Theme Studio is a primary destination absent from the Foundations cards and sidebar; foundation pages show the baseline unless a labelled draft is explicitly selected** | M5, M9 |
| 55 | **Products and modes is absent by default; discovery suggests but never invents products; explicit local, installed, and versioned-manifest sources work, including cross-repository, and are snapshotted into static builds** | M9, M12 |
| 56 | **Navigation, routes, cards, search, and visibility stay synchronized through one page registry; legacy routes redirect; static builds, deep links, assets, redirects, and sharing work under a hosting base path** | M3, M5, M7, M12 |
| 57 | **Missing metadata produces honest unavailable/unknown states; Workbench stays accessible, responsive, lazy-loaded, and isolated; shell and preview appearance are independent** | M5, M8, M11 |
| 58 | Oddkin's own components use one folder per component assembled through the public composition API; ordinary imports never register; migrated components keep their properties, attributes, events, slots, parts, styling, reactivity, lifecycle, cleanup, DOM identity, metadata, exports, and packed behavior; colocated consumer authoring stays supported and tested | M4, M8, M11 |
| 59 | A library built with Oddkin generates its own versioned agent kit from the same metadata, configuration, authored guidance, and examples as its docs and Workbench, without building Workbench; references match its installed packages, prefix, tokens, recipes, and conventions | M4, M5, M11, M12 |
| 60 | Agent-facing facts come from the one metadata model with provenance and verification conditions; unknowns are explicit; authored guidance cites only existing APIs; nothing is inferred from names or types | M4, M8, M10, M11 |
| 61 | Agent context is version-aware: stale artifacts are detected with a refresh path, results state source version and freshness, and capability paths are respected | M5, M11, M12 |
| 62 | CLI and MCP share one deterministic implementation with schema-defined, bounded, structured results; scaffolds and migrations are reviewable dry-run patches with checked preconditions | M11, M12 |
| 63 | Diagnostics are repairable (code, location, subject, expected/received, references, fix only when supported) and distinguish errors, recommendations, and unresolved analysis; preview feedback reports its conditions and never claims complete accessibility | M4, M8, M11 |
| 64 | AI usability claims rest on the recorded four-condition evaluation with held-out tasks and configurations; AI tooling stays optional for every consumer path | M11, M12 |
| 65 | Any number of products (verified at 1, 2, 3, 10) uses one model with stable ids, no count-specific fields or slots, and no single-product ceremony; product identity stays independent of repository, family, design language, theme, mode, density, and profile | M4, M9, M12 |
| 66 | Recomposed definitions are new, frozen, traceable, and never mutate their source; registration conflicts are diagnosed, never silently replaced; both implementations coexist with distinct names/constructors | M4, M8 |
| 67 | Derived definitions and alternative templates pass the same interaction fixtures (properties, methods, events, slots, keyboard, focus, states, forms) and product-specific accessibility checks; foundation results are never presented as certifying a skin | M4, M10, M12 |
| 68 | Product-aware composites render and register product children through one explicit, analyzable mechanism without tag guessing, global substitution, or a provider | M4, M8, M10 |
| 69 | Configuration changes to one product never alter shared presets or other products; generation is deterministic; CSS growth is measured per product without combinatorial emission | M4, M9 |
| 70 | Workbench selection and comparison, on-demand loading and cleanup, and agent lookups stay scoped and usable as the product collection grows; Products and modes stays absent unless enabled | M9, M11 |
| 71 | Minimal foundation consumers load no branded skins, theme generation, tooling, or application services; packed multi-repository product consumers work through public exports | M4, M12 |
| 72 | Impact reports match by stable identity, recognize renames only from authoritative records, classify changes without automatic semver mapping for token values, keep edge provenance and coverage gaps, and widen verification for unresolved dependencies | M5, M8, M9, M12 |
| 73 | Verification results always carry one state (`selected`, `passed`, `failed`, `reused` with conditions, `not-executed`, `unresolved`); untested examples are never shown as passing; visual baselines change only through explicit acceptance | M5, M11, M12 |
| 74 | Portable conformance suites accept legitimate customizations, reject their mutants, use only public capabilities and documented anatomy, and record evidence per implementation, configuration, browser, and suite version; they are not accessibility certification | M4, M8, M10, M12 |
| 75 | Explanations share one model across Workbench, CLI, and agents; declared, potential, and observed relationships stay labelled; no claimed cascade reconstruction, emulated responsiveness, or invented causal history | M9, M11 |
| 76 | Runtime inspection is development-only, opt-in, bounded, owned, invalidated on change, measured for overhead, and absent from production graphs and size budgets | M8, M11, M12 |
| 77 | Runnable reproductions contain only declared inputs, list what needs adapters, use public exports, re-fail in clean directories, and are never published automatically | M11, M12 |
| 78 | Page suspension never disposes the application; restoration resumes declared services without duplicate listeners and revalidates only by policy; persisted restoration is proven, not inferred from history navigation | M7, M8 |
| 79 | DTCG Resolver support is an explicit, evidence-backed decision with documented mappings, never implied by Format support | M9 |
| 80 | The component-definition composer (`compose()`) and the data-composition renderer (`@oddkin/components/composition`) are distinct in prose, imports, examples, and dependency checks | M4, M12 |
| 81 | F01: baseline styles remain usable when enhancements are unavailable | M4, M8 |
| 82 | F02: unsupported modern syntax inside tokens cannot silently remove essential colours or sizing | M4, M9 |
| 83 | F03: nested theme overrides produce the intended semantic values, including dependent aliases | M2, M4, M8 |
| 84 | F04: typed registration preserves inheritance and semantic fallback behavior (tokens and component variables are never registered) | M4, M10 |
| 85 | F05: application overrides behave as documented across layers and Shadow DOM | M4, M8 |
| 86 | F06: any compatibility output preserves cascade and specificity semantics (none ships while the floor needs no lowering) | M8, M12 |
| 87 | F07: components adapt inside narrow containers without viewport changes | M4, M10 |
| 88 | F08: query-container and containment choices never collapse intrinsic layout or clip focus and overlays | M4, M8, M10 |
| 89 | F09: multiple overlays use the correct anchors and clean up fallback observers | M10 |
| 90 | F10: native and fallback overlays keep the intended theme, direction, and interaction behavior | M10 |
| 91 | F11: reduced or unsupported animation leaves controls in the correct final state | M4, M10 |
| 92 | F12: native and fallback field sizing preserve editing behavior | M10 |
| 93 | F13: enhanced native controls retain a fully usable baseline | M10 |
| 94 | F14: forced colours, zoom, text enlargement, and RTL keep controls usable and focus visible | M4, M8, M10 |
| 95 | F15: shared styles work across supported document, Shadow DOM, registration, and delivery scenarios | M3, M4, M12 |
| 96 | F16: production static documentation demonstrates the actual emitted CSS and fallback paths | M4, M11 |
| 97 | Every adopted platform feature is registered with its subfeatures, gate, baseline, and evidence; shipped CSS passes the compatibility lint; the floor and data versions are pinned and re-resolved each release; named floor browsers are run where obtainable and gaps are recorded | M4, M8, M12 |
